- **Charred Leek Oil** (optional, 70 g) — whole leek charred at 120°C/250°F, blended with hot grapeseed oil + Banyuls vinegar, oil decanted.
- **Chestnut Confit** (200 g) — sous vide 90°C/194°F 12 h with pork fat and salt.
- **Sweet-and-Sour Cipolline Onion** (80 g) — sous vide 90°C/194°F 1 h 50 min with vinegar and chestnut honey.
- **Salted Caramel Apple Puree** (200 g) — dark caramel deglazed with cider; apples sous vide 90°C/194°F 20 h in the syrup, then puréed with xanthan gum.
- **Banyuls Glaze** (300 g) — ground pork + trotter roasted, sweated vegetables, **pressure-cooked with Banyuls wine at 1.4 bar/21 psi for 1.5 h**, reduced to a glaze.
- **Chestnut Puffs** (430 g) — choux-style dough (gelatin, butter, bacon fat, chestnut purée) cast, baked, deep-fried until puffed; injected via siphon with Chestnut Cream (300 g, thickened iota carrageenan + konjac gum, whipped in a siphon) and dusted with pain d'épices powder.
- **Pain d'Épices Powder** (300 g) — toasted cinnamon/star anise/clove/aniseed/coriander ground with honey powder, ginger, nutmeg, mace, orange zest.
- **Spiced Ash** (80 g) — pain d'épices powder + carbon color powder + kaolin clay for an edible black "ash," bound with hazelnut oil and N-Zorbit M maltodextrin.
- **Pork Loin Roast** (900 g) — rack cured 48 h in salt + pain d'épices; skin reserved (gelatinized/dried/minced); loin coated in Crisp Coat + Methocel E4M paste, sous vide, **dipped in liquid nitrogen 30 s** (cryosear), dusted in starch, rolled in whipped methylcellulose + minced pork skin, deep-fried 185°C/365°F until puffed.

**Standout technique:** liquid-nitrogen cryosearing of a fully cooked sous vide loin right before a methylcellulose/pork-skin-crusted fry; kaolin clay + carbon powder as edible black "ash"; siphon-foamed prune "coals."

**Plating:** loin rested 3 min, sliced between bones; chestnut puffs filled and dusted; plate finished with warm Banyuls glaze, apple purée, chestnut confit, cipolline onions, prune coals, and bacon; spiced ash dusted over the whole plate.

#### 4. Rack of Lamb with Garlic
*Seaweed tapenade, garlic nougatine, caramelized crème fraîche*

Celebrates *agneau de pré-salé* (salt-marsh lamb), paired with seaweed tapenade and an oyster beignet to evoke coastal terroir; explains that smashing garlic triggers sulfur-compound-producing enzymes that cooking mellows into meatier aromas. **Yield** 4 | **Equipment** sous vide, pressure cooker, whipping siphon | **Time** 12 h.

- **Pickled Garlic** (75 g) — blanched 3 min (deactivates the sulfur-producing enzymes) then pickled 12 h+.
- **Seaweed Tapenade** (525 g) — rehydrated dulse/arame/hijiki + green olives, shallot, caper, anchovy, Dijon, lemon, garlic; thickened with xanthan gum.
- **Caramelized Crème Fraîche** (300 g) — crème fraîche + baking soda **pressure-cooked at 1.4 bar/21 psi for 2.5 h** to force Maillard browning without dry heat; blended with sieved garlic confit and Ultra-Sperse 3.
- **Oyster Beignet** (4) — tapioca/flour/baking-soda/xanthan batter aerated in a siphon (N₂O), coats Kumamoto oysters, deep-fried 200°C/392°F.
- **Garlic Nougatine** (400 g) — glucose syrup DE40 + isomalt with butter, flour, blanched garlic brunoise, Marcona almonds; baked, rolled thin while hot.
- **Rack of Lamb** (400 g) — rosemary-wrapped bones, sous vide 62°C/144°F to core 57°C/135°F (~40 min), rested, seared fat-side down.

**Standout technique:** pressure-cooking crème fraîche with baking soda to force Maillard browning chemically; a savory nougatine (confectionery isomalt technique) built around garlic and almonds.

**Plating:** chops seared fat-side down, sliced between ribs; plated with caramelized crème fraîche, a tapenade quenelle, an oyster beignet, three pickled garlic cloves, warm garlic-rosemary jus, and a garlic nougatine shard; garnished with wild garlic blossoms.

#### 5. Blanquette de Veau
*Veal sweetbreads, constructed cream, kaolin clay, licorice*

Reinterprets the classically "white" braised-veal-and-cream dish by making it "whiter than white" (even the carrots are white): since butter yellows cream sauce, rendered veal marrow fat is instead **homogenized directly into white veal stock** to build a nondairy, kosher-compatible "constructed" veal cream whose fine fat droplets scatter light intensely. **Yield** 4 | **Equipment** sous vide, rotor-stator or ultrahigh-pressure homogenizer | **Time** 13 h.

- **Sous Vide Veal Sweetbreads** (500 g) — milk-soaked 3× over 12 h to purge, sous vide 67°C/153°F 1 h, glazed in a veal-stock/sherry/licorice-root/fennel-seed/star-anise reduction with **kaolin clay**.
- **Rice Crisp** (350 g) — sushi rice cooked to porridge, mixed with glutinous-rice-flour slurry, dehydrated 50°C/122°F 3 h, deep-fried 190°C/375°F.
- **Milk Foam** (500 g) — skim milk sous vide-infused 85°C/185°F 1 h with leek/mushroom/turnip/onion/garlic/thyme; stabilized with sucrose esters (Sucro) and Methocel F50, hand-blended into foam.
- **Glazed White Carrot** (300 g) — sous vide 85°C/185°F 45 min, reduced to a glaze with butter/fructose.
- **Sous Vide Turnip** (200 g) — sous vide 85°C/185°F 20 min with butter.
- **Constructed Veal Cream** (360 g) — tapioca starch, whey protein isolate, iota carrageenan, acetic acid, polysorbate 80, xanthan gum homogenized into cold white veal stock at 85°C/185°F, then rendered veal marrow fat drizzled in while homogenizing until fully emulsified.

**Standout technique:** a polysorbate-80/whey-protein/carrageenan emulsifier system homogenized with veal fat to build a nondairy "cream" sauce via fat-droplet light scattering; kaolin clay as an edible mineral flavoring agent.

**Plating:** sweetbreads basted in the clay-licorice glaze, sliced 2.5 cm thick; plated with milk foam, glazed carrots, turnips, topped with rice crisps; veal cream served alongside.

#### 6. Choucroute Royale
*Alder smoke, gin flavors, Munster, black trumpet mushroom*

Traces sauerkraut fermentation science (*Leuconostoc mesenteroides* colonizing salted, submerged cabbage, converting sugars to lactic acid before other bacteria finish the sour aroma). **Yield** 4 | **Equipment** pressure cooker, vacuum reduction equipment (sous vide/cold smoker optional) | **Time** 72 h (3 weeks if curing your own sauerkraut).

- **Juniper Brine** (600 g) — a gin-botanical brine: juniper, coriander, lemon/bitter-orange zest, angelica root, roasted malt, hops, cinnamon, ginger, allspice.
- **Pork Tenderloin with Juniper** (500 g) — brined 24 h, wrapped in a gelatin-infused leek sheet bonded with **Activa RM (transglutaminase)** to form an edible "skin," steamed, rested 12 h before sous vide.
- **Aromatic Alsatian Mustard** (optional, 245 g) — triple-blanched mustard seed (removes bitterness) + cider vinegar; keeps indefinitely.
- **Smoked Pork Cheek** (240 g) — **cold-smoked at 7°C/45°F, 50% RH (wet-bulb 3°C/37°F), 24 h** with alder/cherry wood, then sous vide 68°C/154°F 48 h.
- **Pork Trotter Beignet** (400 g) — trotter/snout **pressure-cooked at 1 bar/15 psi 2.5 h**, combined with black trumpet mushroom and ham, bound with Methocel K100M, breaded, deep-fried.
- **Braised Sauerkraut** (480 g) — cabbage + rutabaga sauerkraut braised with vegetable stock, dry Riesling, Muscat, and a bay/juniper/clove/coriander sachet.
- **Sweet Potato Fondant** (200 g) — satsuma-imo sous vide 95°C/203°F 45 min.
- **Alsatian Munster Sausage** (1.9 kg) — emulsion-style sausage (pork shoulder/fat ground cold, kept below 5–10°C to prevent overchopping), bound with Insta Cure No. 1, grated Munster, and quatre épices; sous vide to core 59°C/138°F.

**Standout technique:** Activa-bonded gelatin/leek "skin" around the tenderloin; wet-bulb-controlled cold-smoking of the cheek; a gin-botanical brine drawing an explicit parallel between juniper in gin and in pork brines.

**Plating:** sauerkraut spooned into bowls, dressed with sliced sausage, pork cheek, tenderloin, and sweet potato fondant; trotter beignets fried and served alongside with the Alsatian mustard.

**Chapter-wide hydrocolloids/additives:** bromelain, xanthan gum, guar gum, 160 Bloom gelatin, low-acyl gellan (Kelcogel F), sodium citrate, iota/kappa carrageenan, Ultra-Sperse A/3, sucrose esters (Sucro), Methocel F50/E4M/K100M, polysorbate 80, whey protein isolate, kaolin clay, Insta Cure No. 1, Activa RM, N-Zorbit M, konjac gum.

---

### CHAPTER 20 — TOUGH CUTS

*(Three of this chapter's nine dishes — Braised Short Ribs, Osso Buco Milanese, American BBQ — have image-only title spreads that did not extract as text; their full sub-recipes and techniques did extract and are reported below.)*

#### 1. Braised Short Ribs
Component-only extraction (title/headnote not recovered).

- **Braised Short Ribs** (1.2 kg) — four-bone plate short ribs, sous vide **60°C/140°F for 72 h**, peeled off the bone into block portions.
- **Sweet, Sour, and Savory Glaze** (400 g) — beef stock + short-rib juices reduced, infused with cilantro/star anise/Thai basil, finished with tamarind and palm sugar.
- **Crispy Beef Strands** (80 g) — flank steak marinated 48 h (soy/fish sauce/sugar/salt), microwave-dried, pulled into strands, deep-fried to a glassy crisp.
- **Dehydrated Garlic Chip** — elephant garlic blanched in milk, dehydrated.
- **Crispy Beef and Shallot Salad** (100 g) — fried shallot + crispy beef strands + glaze + herbs/lime/chili.

**Standout technique:** 72-hour low-temperature sous vide collagen breakdown; microwave-drying used as a dehydration shortcut for jerky-style beef strands.

#### 2. Pot-au-Feu
*Beef shank rillette, pickled veal tongue, rare flatiron steak, crispy ravigote*

Traces pot-au-feu from its 1867 Larousse-canonized status as the French national dish through reinterpretations by Alain Chapel, Michel Guérard, and Eric Ripert. **Yield** 4 | **Equipment** sous vide, pressure cooker | **Time** 4 days.

- **Glazed Oxtail** (325 g) — sous vide **60°C/140°F for 100 h**; juices reduced to a glaze.
- **Sous Vide Veal Tongue** (250 g) — blanched, sous vide 70°C/158°F 24 h in a cider-vinegar/vegetable brine; liquid reduced to a syrup.
- **Rare Flatiron Steak** (500 g) — two pieces bonded with **Activa RM/GS**, rested 6 h, sous vide 55°C/131°F for 12 h (extendable to 24–48 h for more tenderness).
- **Beignet of Sauce Ravigote** (300 g, inspired by Wylie Dufresne) — a ravigote sauce solidified: milk fluid gel (gelatin + low-acyl gellan) blended with walnut oil, sous vide egg yolk, Dijon, vermouth vinegar, sea beans; cast, cubed, breaded, deep-fried.
- **Beef Shank Rillette** (365 g) — shank sous vide 85°C/185°F 5 h, shredded, folded with an agar/gelatin beef-stock fluid gel and warm rendered suet.
- **Pot-au-Feu Consommé** (200 g) — oxtail/shank roasted with aromatics, **pressure-cooked at 1 bar/15 psi for 2 h**, clarified, reduced, finished with sherry vinegar.
- **Clove Oil** (100 g) — grapeseed oil + 0.5% clove essential oil.
- **Sous Vide Root Vegetables** — baby leek, parisienne carrot/celery root/rutabaga/turnip, pearl onion, each individually sous vide 85°C/185°F 25–40 min.
- **Microwave-Fried Parsley and Carrot Tops** — brushed with oil, microwaved to crisp.

**Standout technique:** Activa-bonded double-thickness flatiron steak; a solid, fried "beignet" version of a classic liquid sauce.

**Plating:** tongue, oxtail, seared flatiron, and warm rillette arranged per plate with root vegetables and fried herb garnish, clove oil drizzle; consommé and beignets served tableside.

#### 3. Hungarian Beef Goulash
*Wagyu beef cheek, sour cream csipetke, compressed dill-pickled vegetables*

Opens with Károly Gundel's warning against foreign chefs "ruining" goulash and lays out gulyás orthodoxy (no flour, no wine, no rice) — which the authors admit to gently bending with black cumin and red wine vinegar. **Yield** 4 | **Equipment** sous vide (centrifuge optional) | **Time** 73 h.

- **Goulash Broth** (1 kg) — tomato confit, red pepper/onion, brown beef stock, toasted caraway/peppercorn, sweet+hot paprika, black cumin, sherry vinegar; sous vide 85°C/185°F 2.5 h, then **centrifuged at 27,500g for 1 h**.
- **American Wagyu Beef Cheek** (450 g) — sous vide **62°C/144°F for 72 h** in the broth.
- **Smoked Pepper Purée** (250 g) — broth + red pepper/onion/ham/smoked paprika sous vide 85°C/185°F 2 h, thickened with Ultra-Sperse 5.
- **Goulash Glaze** (75 g) — broth reduced 400 g → 75 g.
- **Compressed Dill-Pickled Vegetables** (300 g) — red pepper, cucumber, hon-shimeji, pearl onion, individually vacuum-brined.
- **Sour Cream Spaetzle (Csipetke)** — sautéed in clarified butter.
- **Rye Bread** (adapted from Horst Bandel, 1.25 kg) — multi-day sourdough: starter ripened 10–12 h at 21°C/70°F, rye berries sous vide 90°C/194°F 5 h, baked to core 99°C/210°F in a covered cast-iron mold.

**Standout technique:** 27,500g centrifuge clarification of the broth; 72-hour Wagyu cheek.

**Plating:** beef cheek sliced, brushed with glaze, plated over smoked pepper purée; garnished with pickled vegetables; csipetke and toasted rye bread on the side; broth poured tableside.

#### 4. Osso Buco Milanese
Component-only extraction (title/headnote not recovered).

- **Tomato Confit** (adapted from Heston Blumenthal, 150 g) — glycerol-brushed tomatoes dehydrated 110°C→95°C for hours to a deep-red, shriveled confit.
- **Braised Veal Foreshank** (500 g) — whole shank sous vide **62°C/144°F for 72 h**; bones reserved for marrow custard.
- **Bone Marrow Custard** (650 g) — coffee-infused milk + gelatin set with a **dual gellan system** (high-acyl Kelcogel LT100 + low-acyl Kelcogel F) blended into veal stock with garlic confit, cast directly into the marrowbones.
- **Squash Glaze** (150 g) — butternut/onion/carrot juice + veal stock + shank juices, reduced, mounted with butter.
- **Pickled Butternut Squash** (100 g) — vacuum-chamber-infused with a spiced saffron/star-anise/orange-peel brine.
- **Gremolata Crisp** (135 g) — parsley/rice purée siphon-dispensed and microwave-dehydrated into a crisp sheet.
- **Tomato Vinegar Foam** (125 g) — saffron-vinegar tomato water stabilized with whey protein isolate + soy lecithin.
- **Braised Pine Nuts with Winter Squash** (500 g) — kabocha/veal-stock/wine reduction with pine nuts **pressure-cooked 1 bar/15 psi 7 min**; kuri squash sous vide 90°C/194°F 1.25 h, puréed with Parmigiano and saffron.

**Standout technique:** dual acyl-gellan bone marrow custard cast directly into real marrowbones; siphon-aerated, microwave-dried "crisp" gremolata.

#### 5. American BBQ
Component-only extraction (title/headnote not recovered) — a full regional survey.

- **Rubs:** Memphis Rub, Kansas Rub.
- **Regional sauces:** Kansas City (ketchup-based), East Texas (ancho-chili/onion-butter), Memphis (tomato/cider vinegar/Worcestershire), North Carolina Eastern (vinegar-only, no tomato), South Carolina (Dijon-mustard/lager base), Kentucky (ketchup/molasses/pineapple/bourbon, for mutton), North Carolina Lexington-style (vinegar+ketchup dip), and a **Centrifuged BBQ Sauce** (tomato confit + beef jus, **centrifuged at 27,500g 1 h**, decanted clear). Sidebar: 0.2% xanthan gum improves sauce cling to meat.
- **Proteins**, all smoked then sous vide-finished: **Pulled Pork Shoulder** (smoked 65°C/149°F, 60% RH, 7 h; sous vide 65°C/150°F 72 h); **Pork Ribs** (same smoke; sous vide 60°C/140°F 48 h; torch-flashed); **Beef Short Ribs** (same smoke; sous vide 60°C/140°F 72 h); **Beef Brisket** (same smoke; sous vide 63°C/146°F 72 h — noted for its "highly developed smoke ring").
- **Sides:** Baked Beans (navy beans pressure-cooked 1 bar/15 psi 25 min, then sous vide-sauced), Buttermilk Biscuits, Red/White Coleslaw (the white version rotor-stator-homogenized), Potato Salad, Fried Green Tomatoes, Fried Pickles (Trisol-thickened batter), Remoulade, Corn Bread (part-sweetened with isomalt).

**Standout technique:** wet-bulb-controlled smoking for every protein before a multi-day sous vide finish; centrifuge-clarified BBQ sauce.

#### 6. Cassoulet Toulousain — Autumn
*Tarbais beans, crispy duck tongues, Pyrenees ham infusion*

Cassoulet as fiercely regional dish (three rival southwestern French towns each claim the "true" version); pressure-cooking the Tarbais beans with controlled salt keeps interiors creamy without splitting the skins. **Yield** 4 | **Equipment** sous vide, centrifuge, pressure cooker | **Time** 55 h.

- **Toulousain Garlic Sausage** (750 g) — pork+fatback (Activa TI can replace overnight presalting), sous vide to core 58°C/136°F, held 30 min to pasteurize.
- **Sous Vide Duck Confit Pavé** (850 g) — duck legs cured, dusted with **Activa RM**, packed skin-out into a mold, sous vide **60°C/140°F for 48 h** — confit reengineered as a bonded pressed terrine.
- **Pressure-Cooked Tarbais Beans** (200 g) — with **calcium chloride** for skin control, pressure-cooked 1 bar/15 psi 45 min; can be canned/retort-packed for shelf stability.
- **Braised Lamb Shoulder** (500 g) — sous vide 56°C/133°F 48 h.
- **Crispy Duck Tongues** (65 g) — sous vide 88°C/190°F 5 h, dehydrated, deep-fried 205°C/400°F until puffed.
- **Cured Ham Broth** (350 g) — pressure-cooked 1 bar/15 psi 1 h.

**Standout technique:** calcium chloride for bean-skin texture control; Activa-bonded duck confit terrine.

#### 7. Cassoulet Toulousain — Spring
*Fresh shell beans, duck ham, spring garlic consommé*

A lighter, cold-served counterpart built around fresh shell beans, milk-fed lamb, and green garlic — about a quarter of the autumn version's time. **Yield** 4 | **Equipment** sous vide, pressure cooker (centrifuge optional) | **Time** 20 h. Served cold.

- **Green Garlic and Pork Sausage** (300 g) — bound with **Nutrifos 088** phosphate, shaped without casings, sous vide to core 60°C/140°F.
- **Sous Vide Duck Ham** (adapted from Wylie Dufresne, 750 g) — cured with Insta Cure No. 1, bonded with Activa GS/RM slurry, pressed into a terrine, sous vide 69°C/156°F 6 h.
- **Spring Garlic Consommé** (250 g) — duck/vegetable base pressure-cooked, **double-clarified by centrifuge (27,500g, 1 h)** — once for the base, once for fresh green-garlic juice.
- **Country Bread Crisps** (100 g) — brioche brushed with duck fat, baked.
- **Poached Lamb Loin** (320 g) — sous vide 58°C/136°F to core 57°C/135°F.
- **Fresh Bean Salad** (100 g) — fava/pea/Romano/wax beans individually sous vide 88°C/190°F 20 min, dressed with hazelnut oil.

**Standout technique:** double-centrifugation for an ultra-clear consommé; Nutrifos phosphate binder.

#### 8. Historic Lamb Curries
*A culinary tour of regional Indian curries*

Five regional Indian curry styles built on a shared lamb-shank base, chosen to reflect India's crossroads history (Mughal, Portuguese, British influence): Mughal (north), Muslim/Hyderabadi, Goan (vindaloo), Kerala/Malayali, and Masala/Madras. Sauce and protein are cooked separately for mix-and-match flexibility. **Yield** 4 | **Equipment** sous vide (centrifuge optional) | **Time** 60 h. *Choose one curry style per service.*

Base: **Sous Vide Lamb Shank** (reheated 58°C/136°F) + **Tomato Purée**.

1. **Mughal** — almond-cashew-poppy-seed sauce with toasted whole spices, raisins, yogurt; sides of Cashew Halvah (N-Zorbit M-thickened) and Candied Orange and Carrot Pulao.
2. **Muslim** — onion-ginger-garlic sauce with cream, rosewater, saffron (0.2% xanthan gum optional); sides include pulao, dates, and a Saffron Honey Foam (soy lecithin + whey protein isolate).
3. **Goan (Vindaloo)** — onion-tomato base with mustard seed, cardamom, turmeric, fennel, cumin, cane vinegar; sides of Cucumber Black-Eyed Pea Salad and papadams.
4. **Kerala (Malayali)** — shallot-chili-ginger-garlic base finished with coconut milk; sides of Green Papaya Pickle, Crispy Okra, and Raw Okra with chaat masala.
5. **Masala (Madras)** — onion-tomato-coconut with tamarind, toasted fenugreek/coriander/fennel; sides of Banana Yogurt (banana juice extracted by **centrifuge at 27,500g 1 h**), sous vide-extracted Tamarind Paste, and Stuffed Smoky Braised Eggplant.

**Standout technique:** centrifuge used for flavor-extraction (clear banana juice) rather than stock clarification; sous vide tamarind extraction.

#### 9. Sunday Pork Belly
*Applesauce terrine, crispy corn pudding, red wine cabbage, red-eye gravy*

A gelled applesauce "terrine" is grilled to caramelize its sugars; corn pudding is fried like a beignet; the belly's curing-nitrite brine prevents myoglobin oxidation (graying); red-eye gravy is rebuilt from ham-drippings-plus-coffee logic — noting salt, not sugar, is what actually masks coffee's bitterness. **Yield** 4 | **Equipment** sous vide, juicer, pressure cooker (freeze dryer optional) | **Time** 80 days (dominated by a multi-day cure).

- **Sous Vide Pork Belly** (1.6 kg) — pink-brine injected, cured 3 days, redrained/resealed 2 more days, sous vide **62°C/144°F for 40 h**.
- **Red-Eye Gravy** (75 g) — pork stock + maple syrup, infused with whole coffee beans, thickened with **microcrystalline cellulose (Avicel CC-200)** instead of a roux.
- **Hot Applesauce Terrine** (600 g) — clarified apple juice set with agar + locust bean gum + sorbitol — a heat-stable gel that survives grilling/warming intact.
- **Crispy Corn Pudding** (780 g) — popcorn-steeped milk custard, breaded in freeze-dried-corn/panko/Ultra-Crisp, deep-fried.
- **Red Wine Cabbage** (375 g) — blanched sous vide 90°C/194°F 4 min, finished in a red-wine/cabbage-juice/pineapple-juice reduction.

**Standout technique:** microcrystalline cellulose as a non-starch gravy thickener; a fruit terrine engineered with agar/locust-bean-gum/sorbitol specifically to survive grilling without melting.

**Chapter-wide hydrocolloids/additives:** Activa RM/GS/TI, calcium chloride, Nutrifos 088, Insta Cure No. 1, low/high-acyl gellan (Kelcogel F/LT100), agar, 160 Bloom gelatin, Ultra-Sperse 3/5, whey protein isolate, soy lecithin, microcrystalline cellulose (Avicel), locust bean gum, sorbitol, N-Zorbit M, Trisol, isomalt, xanthan gum.

---

### CHAPTER 21 — POULTRY

#### 1. Foie Gras à la Vapeur — *inspired by Alain Senderens*
*Napa cabbage, green walnuts, Muscat grapes, and vanilla*

Homage to Senderens's early-1980s pioneering steamed-foie-gras-in-cabbage (the leaf acting as a natural sous vide bag) and to his *mariage des vins et des mets* wine-pairing philosophy — evoking Sauternes character via dry white port, vanilla, and bay laurel without adding sugar. **Yield** 4 | **Equipment** sous vide, pressure cooker, blowtorch (centrifuge optional) | **Time** 40 h.

- **Sous Vide Foie Gras** (400 g) — cooked directly from frozen at 53°C/127°F to core 52°C/126°F (~35 min), then torch-seared.
- **Duck Broth with Vanilla and Bay Leaf** — browned duck, **pressure-cooked at 1 bar/15 psi 1.5 h** with white port, white wine, vanilla, bay leaf; explicitly *not vented*, to keep volatile aromatics.
- **Compressed Muscat Grapes** (100 g) — vacuum-compressed with clarified grape juice, verjuice, fructose, malic acid.
- **Braised Napa Cabbage** (250 g) — sautéed in rendered foie gras fat.
- **Spiced Walnut Vinegar Gel** (200 g) — duck stock set with **agar (3%)** plus ascorbic acid, toasted peppercorn/coriander/Sichuan pepper; grated tableside as a savory "snow."

**Standout technique:** cooking foie gras directly from frozen to a tight 52°C core; a pressure-cooked, deliberately unvented aromatic broth.

**Plating:** cabbage mounded center-plate, sliced foie gras on top, garnished with grapes, shaved green walnut, crushed Sichuan pepper; walnut gel grated over; broth poured tableside.

#### 2. Crispy Hay-Smoked Chicken
*Pickled ramps, fiddleheads, sticky rice, vin jaune*

Smoking normally toughens chicken skin into leather; this recipe uses a fast, light hay-smoke plus a separately prepared "Ultracrisp" skin **welded back onto the roasted bird with Activa (transglutaminase)**, then cryosears in liquid nitrogen before frying — a dish built entirely on Modernist technique despite looking traditional. **Yield** 4 | **Equipment** blowtorch, pressure cooker, sous vide (combi oven/Pacojet/LN₂ optional) | **Time** 16 h.

- **Hay-Smoked Chicken Crown** (1 kg) — brined 12 h, smoked by igniting hay/straw under a covered pan 10 min; skin removed and discarded.
- **Crisp Chicken Crown** (1.25 kg) — Ultracrisp skin dusted with **Activa RM/GS**, applied over the crown, vacuum-sealed, refrigerated 5+ h to bond.
- **Sauce Vin Jaune** (200 g) — stock + reduced Vin Jaune du Jura, emulsified with rendered chicken fat using **Propylene Glycol Alginate**.
- **Stuffed Morels** (250 g) — foie gras parfait + rehydrated morel farce piped into fresh morels; soaking liquid reduced into a glaze.
- **Pickled Ramps** (450 g) — hot vinegar/sugar/salt brine.
- **Stir-Fried Fiddlehead Ferns** (155 g) — high-heat with bacon, garlic, scallion.
- **Steamed Sticky Rice** (150 g) — finished with coconut milk/butter.

**Standout technique:** Activa-welded separately-crisped skin reattached to roasted meat; liquid-nitrogen cryosear between fry cycles; Propylene Glycol Alginate as a fat emulsifier in a pan sauce.

**Plating:** crown cryoseared, deep-fried 3 cycles until golden; breast sliced over stir-fried fiddleheads, garnished with morels and pickled ramps; sauce vin jaune and sticky rice on the side.

#### 3. Duck Apicius — *homage to Alain Senderens's "Canard Apicius"*
*Quince jelly, spiced honey glass, saffron turnip, pomegranate jus*

Traces back to the ancient Roman gourmet Apicius and *garum* (fermented fish sauce) — modern bagoong/nam pla substitute for it — combined with pomegranate in the jus. **Yield** 4 | **Equipment** refractometer, pH meter, pressure cooker, sous vide (Thermomix optional) | **Time** 6 h.

- **Pomegranate and Garum Jus** (550 g) — pomegranate seeds crushed inside a vacuum bag (clean juicing), combined with duck stock and garum; malt vinegar dosed to hit **pH 4.5**; thickened with Ultra-Sperse 3.
- **Date Puree** (400 g) — dates/ginger juice/cardamom sous vide 80°C/176°F 2 h.
- **Quince Jelly** — formed into rectangles.
- **Spiced Honey Glass** (365 g) — spray-dried honey + **trehalose** (defeats honey's hygroscopicity) melted 160°C/320°F, ground, remelted into thin glass sheets, stored with silica gel.
- **Braised Turnips with Saffron** (200 g) — sous vide 85°C/185°F 35 min.
- **Duck Breast** — sous vide 56°C/133°F to core 55°C/131°F, cryoseared or pan-seared.

**Standout technique:** pH-targeted sauce (refractometer/pH meter specified equipment); trehalose combined with honey specifically to allow a crisp sugar "glass."

**Plating:** turnips, date purée, and warmed quince jelly on the plate; sliced duck breast on top; finished with pomegranate-garum jus and a honey-glass shard.

#### 4. Pigeon en Salmis
*Blood pudding, brioche, grain ragout, celery root*

A three-course tasting built almost entirely from one bird, meditating on Victorian frugality with luxe ingredients — course 1 turns pigeon blood into a *boudin noir* custard soaking day-old brioche (pain perdu); course 2 sautés offal over a wild-grain ragout; course 3 slices the breast with a farce royale in a warm oat jus. **Yield** 4 | **Equipment** Pacojet, pressure cooker, sous vide | **Time** 13 h.

*Course 1:* Boudin Noir Custard (pigeon blood/yolk/cream/gelatin sous vide 70°C/158°F 1 h) soaks Boudin Noir Bread Pudding (vacuum-forced absorption into brioche, fried, glucose-glazed); Foie Gras and Hazelnut Ganache (Pacojet-spun, stabilized with microcrystalline cellulose, cured with Insta Cure No. 1); Maple Vinegar Gastrique; Candied Puffed Oats.

*Course 2:* Ragout of Grains (spelt, barley, quinoa, sprouted brown rice, each **pressure-cooked separately** for individually optimal texture); Sous Vide Pigeon Offal (hearts/kidneys 70°C/158°F 20 min; gizzards 60°C/140°F 12 h); Madeira Gelée (set with a **low-acyl + high-acyl gellan blend**); Pickled Jerusalem Artichokes.

*Course 3:* Farce Royale (pigeon leg meat bound with gelatin, Insta Cure No. 1, and **Nutrifos 088**); Roast Pigeon Crown (skin reattached over the farce with **Activa RM/GS**); Toasted Oat Jus (pressure-cooked 1.25 h with steel-cut oats); Pickled Celery Root; Celery Root Mousseline.

**Standout technique:** dual-gellan (low-acyl/high-acyl) gelée texture engineering; per-grain pressure-cooking timetable; Activa used twice in the same dish (skin-to-farce bonding).

**Plating (3 courses):** fried bread pudding + ganache quenelle + gastrique; grain ragout topped with browned offal, gelée cubes, pickled artichoke, shaved mushroom; celery root mousseline topped with sliced roast breast and warm oat jus.

#### 5. Guinea Hen Tajine
*Preserved figs, navel oranges, purple Beldi olives*

Argues the traditional conical *tagine slaoui* lid (which traps vaporizing juices to raise humidity/wet-bulb temperature) is not required — sous vide achieves the same moist result with far more precise control. **Yield** 4 | **Equipment** dehydrator, sous vide, pressure cooker, griddle (optional/mixed) | **Time** 5 h (4 weeks if curing your own preserved lemon).

- **Pickled Figs** (400 g) — dried black figs sous vide 80°C/176°F 4 h with vinegars and warm spices.
- **Tagine Base** (150 g) — sautéed onion + reduced chicken stock, ginger juice, garlic, lemon, honey, cinnamon, preserved lemon, pickled fig, saffron.
- **Sous Vide Guinea Hen** (250 g) — breast and thigh marinated separately in **live-culture Greek yogurt** (deliberately inoculating the meat surface with lactic acid bacteria, tandoori-style), then sous vide separately: thigh 62°C/144°F 5 h; breast 55°C/131°F to core 54°C/129°F, held 12 min for pasteurization.
- **Puffed Chickpea Salad** ("inspired by Wylie Dufresne," 150 g) — pressure-cooked, dehydrated 50°C/122°F, deep-fried to puff; dressed with tahini, lemon, sumac, mint.
- **Navel Orange Salad** (80 g) — supremed orange, red onion, Beldi olive, cumin, argan oil, orange blossom water.
- **Moroccan Batbout Flatbread** (725 g) — turmeric/golden-oregano dough, panfried at assembly.

**Standout technique:** deliberate live-yogurt-culture marination as an applied-microbiology flavor step; sous vide reframed explicitly as a controllable substitute for the tagine's own humidity mechanism.

**Plating:** sliced guinea hen with tagine base spooned over; garnished with cumin, parsley, preserved lemon, pickled fig, toasted almond; orange salad, puffed chickpea salad, and flatbread served alongside.

**Chapter-wide hydrocolloids/additives:** agar, Activa RM/GS, Propylene Glycol Alginate, Ultra-Sperse 3, trehalose, microcrystalline cellulose (Avicel CG200), low-acyl + high-acyl gellan (Kelcogel F/LT100), Insta Cure No. 1, Nutrifos 088, 160 Bloom gelatin.

---

### CHAPTER 22 — FISH

#### 1. Fish and Chips
*Malt vinegar powder, pommes soufflées, sea urchin tartar*

Batter absorbs frying heat before the fish does, moderating its cooking rate; alcohol (vodka) evaporates faster than water for a gently cooked interior and quickly crisped crust; an N₂O-whipped siphon foam batter creates millions of weak spots so the crust shatters rather than chews tough. **Yield** 4 | **Equipment** (optional) sous vide, whipping siphon, spray dryer | **Time** 12 h.

- **Malt Vinegar Powder** (optional, 70 g) — spray-dried for a concentrated acid dust.
- **Halibut Cheeks** (400 g) skewered and trimmed, dipped 3× in a hot (95°C/203°F) **dual-gellan gel** (low-acyl + high-acyl + sodium citrate) to build a set shell before battering.
- **Siphoned Tempura Batter** (860 g) — flour/rice flour/vodka/malt syrup whipped in an N₂O siphon; fish dusted with Trisol first for adhesion.
- **Sea Urchin Tartar Sauce** (300 g) — sous vide egg (65°C/149°F, 30 min) mayonnaise with sea urchin tongue.
- **Pommes Soufflées** (350 g) — potato ovals parcooked 143°C/290°F until they blister/puff (needs 19–22% potato dry matter to work), finished with a second fry.

**Standout technique:** hot dual-gellan gel dip as a protective pre-batter shell; N₂O-whipped foam batter.

**Plating:** cheeks fried ~4 min, rotated and drizzled with extra batter for a lacy web crust; dusted with vinegar powder; plated with pommes soufflées and tartar sauce.

#### 2. Hamachi Maltaise
*Blood orange, asparagus, egg yolk, spaghetti squash*

Classic sauce Maltaise (hollandaise + blood orange) rebuilt as a sabayon stabilized with an egg-yolk fluid gel and dispensed from a siphon, paired with seared hamachi. **Yield** 4 | **Equipment** sous vide, whipping siphon | **Time** 1 h 20 min.

- **Blood Orange Sabayon** (250 g) — shallot/wine/blood-orange reduction + sous vide egg yolk (65°C/149°F, 35 min) + spray-dried orange juice + xanthan gum (0.08%); loaded into an N₂O siphon.
- **Confit Egg Yolk Puree** (110 g) — sous vide 69°C/156°F 45 min, puréed with champagne vinegar and glucose syrup.
- **Sous Vide Green Asparagus** (85°C/185°F, 10 min) and **White Asparagus** (89°C/192°F, ~7 min, seared cut-side down).
- **Marinated Spaghetti Squash** (150 g) — shredded, marinated with orange-honey-vinegar dressing dosed with **acetaldehyde** for a "fresh-squeezed" citrus aroma boost.
- **Seared Hamachi** (500 g) — one side seared to core 32°C/90°F, basted with brown butter.

**Standout technique:** xanthan-stabilized citrus sabayon aerated in an N₂O siphon; precision-dosed acetaldehyde for aroma enhancement.

**Plating:** hamachi and both asparagus types broiled briefly to warm; dotted with confit yolk; garnished with marinated squash and blood orange supremes; sabayon dispensed from the siphon tableside.

#### 3. Monkfish with Mediterranean Flavors
*Zucchini blossom beignet with halibut brandade filling, spice mix emulsion, mussels*

Monkfish's lobster-like tail texture ("poor man's lobster") is cooked sous vide and garnished with a zucchini blossom stuffed with halibut brandade. **Yield** 4 | **Equipment** sous vide, whipping siphon | **Time** 49 h (or 15 days if curing salt cod from scratch).

- **Pâte à Choux** (725 g) — Methocel SGA150-hydrated milk base plus classic choux method.
- **Halibut Brandade** (~800 g) — salted halibut soaked 48 h total, sous vide 58°C/135°F, combined with sous vide riced potato.
- **Zucchini Blossom Beignets** (650 g) — brandade + choux + carbonated water whipped in an N₂O siphon to stiff peaks, piped into blossoms, deep-fried.
- **Sous Vide Mussels** (450 g) — blanched 3 min, marinated with shallot, fennel, wine, pastis, preserved lemon.
- **Spice-Mix Emulsion** (250 g) — mussel juice + Manzanilla sherry reduction, xanthan-emulsified with olive oil and a fish spice mix (hazelnut, sesame, coriander, poppy, ginger, chamomile).
- **Sous Vide Monkfish Pavé** (450 g) — vacuum-sealed with overlapping baby zucchini slices arranged like fish scales, sous vide **48°C/119°F to core 47°C/117°F** (~25 min).

**Standout technique:** vacuum-sealed zucchini "fish scales" laid onto the fillet surface for a visual effect; whipped-siphon-aerated choux/brandade batter.

**Plating:** pavé plated with the beignet, cooked mussels, green almonds, extra spice mix dusted on; emulsion poured tableside.

#### 4. Skate in Black Butter
*Black butter, caper droplets, Meyer lemon, toasted bread*

Skate wings taper unevenly; the recipe adopts Wylie Dufresne's solution of Activa-bonding two wings into a uniform pavé. Classic brown-butter-and-caper skate is reinterpreted with spherified capers and carbonated raisins. **Yield** 4 | **Equipment** sous vide, whipping siphon | **Time** 8 h.

- **Black Butter Emulsion** (300 g) — reduced fish stock blended with black butter, soy sauce, and **squid ink**; mounted with butter, white miso, lemon.
- **Caper Droplets** (100 g) — caper purée + calcium lactate dropped into a **sodium alginate bath** — explicitly modeled on El Bulli's "liquid olives" spherification.
- **Carbonated Golden Raisins** (100 g) — verjuice-hydrated raisins charged in a carbonating siphon (3× CO₂) for 1.5 h.
- **Skate Pavé with Bread Crust** (480 g) — two wings dusted with **Activa RM**, stacked and bonded 6+ h, topped with a bread square, seared bread-side-down.

**Standout technique:** Activa-bonded double-wing pavé; alginate/calcium-lactate spherified caper "droplets"; CO₂-carbonated raisins.

**Plating:** skate seared bread-crust-side down; lemon curd spooned across the plate, fish centered, garnished with caper droplets and carbonated raisins; brown butter fumet and black butter emulsion served on the side.

#### 5. Salmon Rus
*Salmon oil, smoked butter, root vegetables, cryopoached dill meringue*

Traces cured salmon to Viking "Rus" traders; salmon is lightly cured then cooked sous vide at very low temperature; a dill-vodka meringue is cryopoached in liquid nitrogen as a palate cleanser. **Yield** 4 | **Equipment** sous vide, Dewar bowl, liquid nitrogen, whipping siphon (smoking equipment optional) | **Time** 25 h.

- **Sous Vide Salmon** (400 g) — brined 7 h, sous vide **41°C/106°F to core 40°C/104°F** (~25 min).
- **Puffed Salmon Skin Pillows** (35 g) — skin sous vide 85°C/185°F 3 h, edges bonded with an **Activa GS** slurry into sealed "balloons," deep-fried until puffed.
- **Root Vegetable Jus** (350 g) — salmon-belly trim + juiced turnip/rutabaga/celery root/onion/leek/parsnip, sous vide 85°C/185°F 3 h, reduced, finished with cold-smoked butter.
- **Carrot and Dill Fricassee** (125 g) — sous vide 85°C/185°F 40 min with ajowan seed.
- **Cryopoached Dill Meringue** (inspired by Heston Blumenthal, 1.2 kg) — isomalt + LM pectin syrup infused with dill/cucumber/lime; egg-white-and-vodka meringue whipped in an N₂O siphon to a stiff, glossy texture, dispensed onto a spoon and **cryopoached in liquid nitrogen, max 10 s** (explicit safety warning against longer freezing — can burn a diner's lip/tongue) for crisp-outside/soft-inside texture.

**Standout technique:** liquid-nitrogen cryopoaching of a whipped meringue with an explicit safety limit; Activa-sealed puffed salmon-skin pillows.

**Plating:** meringue served first as a palate cleanser; salmon and fricassee plated with salmon oil cream dots, roe, dill, smoked salt; skin pillows on the side; root vegetable jus poured tableside.

#### 6. Malaysian Steamed Snapper
*Crisped skin, banana leaf, fermented prawn, Malaysian aromatic oil*

Banana-leaf steaming as a self-contained parcel mimicking sous vide humidity control; a shrimp-noodle sheet is explicitly inspired by Wylie Dufresne's Activa-bonded shrimp extrusions. **Yield** 4 | **Equipment** sous vide (dehydrator/combi oven optional) | **Time** 13 h.

- **Crispy Snapper Skin** (50 g) — sous vide 85°C/185°F 3 h, brushed with egg white + maltodextrin, dehydrated 50°C/122°F ~5 h.
- **Malaysian Aromatic Oil** (500 g) — grapeseed oil simmered with tomato, shallot, Sichuan chili, **belacan (shrimp paste)**, ginger, bird's-eye chili, rested 12 h.
- **Fermented Shrimp Sheets** (inspired by Wylie Dufresne, 300 g) — shrimp purée + coconut milk + belacan + **Activa TI/RM**, spread thin, steamed at 55°C/131°F to activate the enzyme, then finished at 100°C/212°F — a two-stage cross-linking process.
- **Wok-fried Malaysian Vegetables** (200 g) — bamboo shoot, lotus root, fermented prawn sheet, garlic chives, cabbage, bean sprouts.
- **Sous Vide Snapper** (350 g) — glazed with sambal badjak/kecap manis/Shaoxing wine, sous vide 52°C/125°F to core 51°C/123°F (~20 min).
- **Green Mango and Cashew Salad** (256 g) — cashew-coconut dressing with xanthan gum (0.28%).

**Standout technique:** two-stage Activa activation (55°C then 100°C) to set a raw shrimp purée into a firm "noodle sheet."

**Plating:** wok-fried vegetables topped with the snapper, finished with mango-cashew salad, crispy skin, mint/cilantro.

#### 7. Black Cod "Fredy Girardet"
*(Title spread not extracted; sub-recipes recovered.)*

- **Blossom Salad** (125 g) — shaved baby artichoke, apricot julienne, organic rose petals, pansies, wild wood violets.
- **Condrieu Butter** (340 g) — Condrieu wine **vacuum-reduced** (preserving floral aromatics that heat would degrade) to 150 g, mounted with butter, finished with osmanthus vinegar and a trace of orange blossom/rose essential oil.
- **Artichoke Confit** (150 g) — sous vide 85°C/185°F 1.5 h.
- **Fricassee of Fava Beans and Lychees** (150 g).
- **Apricot and Jasmine Puree** (250 g) — thickened with xanthan gum, flavored with a **single drop** of jasmine essential oil.
- Black cod dry-cured with salt/sugar.

**Standout technique:** vacuum reduction of Condrieu wine to concentrate floral aromatics without cooking them off; trace/single-drop essential-oil dosing.

#### 8. Hawaiian Poke
*Escolar and tuna checkerboard, ogo, candlenut, passion fruit*

Traces poke from Polynesian raw-reef-fish tradition through sugarcane-era immigration to the modern ahi-and-ogo standard; reimagines yellowfin tuna checkerboarded with escolar. **Yield** 4 | **Equipment** (optional) Pacojet | **Time** 12 h.

- **Tuna and Escolar Checkerboard** (425 g) — yellowfin/albacore (explicitly not bluefin — sidebar notes bluefin populations fell 97% since the early 1960s) and escolar bonded with an **Activa RM** slurry, stacked into a 5×5 checkerboard, bonded 4+ h.
- **Passion Fruit Granita** (180 g) — Pacojet-processed.
- **Roasted Candlenuts** (50 g) — roasted (candlenuts are mildly toxic raw).
- **Marinated Ogo** (25 g) — lime, mirin, tamari, sesame oil.
- **Sweet Onion Salsa** (40 g).

**Standout technique:** Activa-bonded raw-fish checkerboard. Food-safety sidebars: escolar's waxy esters cap a serving at 100 g; raw candlenuts are toxic.

**Plating:** checkerboard sliced 3 mm thick, topped with granita, ogo, and salsa; garnished with jicama, cilantro, passion fruit seed, grated candlenut, sesame oil.

**Chapter-wide hydrocolloids/additives:** low/high-acyl gellan (Kelcogel F/LT100), sodium citrate, xanthan gum, Methocel SGA150, sodium alginate + calcium lactate, Activa RM/GS/TI (used in half the chapter's dishes), maltodextrin, isomalt + LM pectin, glucose syrup, Trisol.

---

### CHAPTER 23 — SHELLFISH

#### 1. Shrimp Cocktail
*(Title spread not extracted; components recovered.)*

- **Passion Fruit Brown Butter Fluid Gel** (450 g) — passion fruit juice set with **agar**, blended into a fluid gel with grated horseradish, melted brown butter, malic acid — a Modernist stand-in for classic horseradish cocktail sauce.
- **Passion Fruit–White Soy Sauce Vinaigrette** (70 g).
- **Sous Vide Baby Beet** (375 g) — sous vide 88°C/190°F 1 h, re-sealed in its own cooking juices.
- **Steamed Live Spot Prawn** (720 g) — steamed 90°C/195°F, 100% humidity, 3 min (or sous vide alternative at 45°C/114°F, 20 min).

**Standout technique:** agar fluid-gel cocktail sauce; a very low (45°C) sous vide option for delicate prawn doneness.

#### 2. Lobster Américaine
*(Title spread not extracted; components and full assembly recovered.)*

- **Shellfish Stock** (800 g) — chicken/pork roasted; whole Dungeness crab crushed and fried to render crab oil; aromatics fried in that oil; spice sachet; **pressure-cooked at 1 bar/15 psi for 1 h**.
- **Glazed Carrot** (120 g) — sous vide 80°C/176°F 40 min with carotene butter and a mandarin-honey glaze.
- **Lobster Mushroom Confit** (200 g) — *Hypomyces lactifluorum* (a fungus that turns host mushrooms lobster-red) sous vide with carotene butter; a vegetarian plate variant uses only the mushroom.
- **Poached Lobster Tail** (240 g) — sous vide 52°C/125°F to core 51°C/123°F.
- **Coconut-Lobster Emulsion** (90 g) — reduced shellfish stock + coconut cream powder, **locust bean gum (0.15%) + whey protein isolate (0.4%)** — "locust bean gum gives body, while whey protein isolate aids in foaming... a velvety, dense foam."

**Standout technique:** locust-bean-gum/whey-protein-isolate combination engineered specifically for foam density and stability.

**Plating:** three seared lobster medallions per plate beside a glazed carrot; garnished with mandarin, mushroom-confit slices, foam (dispensed with an espresso steam wand or milk frother), kinome, coconut cream powder; optional grated freeze-dried lobster tail.

#### 3. Thai Crab Miang
*Frozen pomelo, puffed crab, Makrud lime*

Riffs on Bangkok street food *miang kham*, inspired by David Thompson's version at Nahm (London); swaps sweet Dungeness crab for dried shrimp, served with crisp crab crackers in an American "chips and dip" spirit. **Yield** 4 | **Equipment** dehydrator (meat slicer/LN₂ optional) | **Time** 8 h.

- **Puffed Crab Crackers** (125 g) — a tapioca-starch "shrimp-chip" dough steamed, sliced 1 mm, dehydrated, deep-fried to puff.
- **Crab Oil** (400 g) — shell and liver deep-fried 1 h to extract pigment/flavor.
- **Miang Paste** (185 g) — galangal, roasted coconut, peanut, lemongrass, chili, roasted fermented shrimp paste (gapi), Makrud lime leaf.
- **Frozen Pomelo Cells** (100 g) — **flash-frozen with liquid nitrogen** to fracture cell walls for clean juice-sac separation.
- **Marinated Crab Salad** (325 g).

**Standout technique:** liquid-nitrogen freezing of pomelo cells for clean separation.

**Plating:** dressed crab salad on betel leaves with crackers alongside.

#### 4. Pulpo a la Gallega
*Chorizo powder, baked potato, black olive*

Debunks the wine-cork octopus-tenderizing myth; explains toughness comes from the constrictor muscle and that sous vide is ideal for melting collagen into gelatin over a long, moist cook. **Yield** 4 | **Equipment** sous vide, dehydrator, whipping siphon, griddle | **Time** 24 h (73 h if curing fresh chorizo).

- **Sous Vide Octopus** (450 g) — blanched, sous vide **85°C/185°F for 4 h**; half peeled/tender, half left unpeeled for a charred texture.
- **Chorizo Crisps** (200 g) — N-Zorbit M-dusted, dehydrated.
- **Frozen Chorizo Powder** (250 g) — chorizo frozen solid (or flash-frozen with liquid nitrogen) for fine grating.
- **Black Olive Oil** (adapted from Michel Bras, 100 g) — dehydrated black olives blended into oil until it turns black.
- **Smoked Potato Confit** (250 g); Baked Potato Foam (N₂O-siphon whipped, held at 70°C/158°F).

**Standout technique:** liquid-nitrogen-frozen chorizo grated tableside like a "snow"; two textures from one octopus batch (peeled/tender vs. charred/unpeeled).

**Plating:** peeled and grilled unpeeled octopus legs plated with potato confit and chorizo crisps; frozen chorizo grated over tableside; olive oil drizzle, pimentón dust, potato foam on the side.

#### 5. Shellfish Omakase
*Crustacean jewels of the Pacific Northwest*

An 8-course chef's-choice tasting celebrating Pacific Northwest shellfish, framed by Japanese *washoku* philosophy (five colors/tastes/senses/methods/outlooks). **Yield** 4 | **Equipment** sous vide, carbonation equipment, shabu-shabu pot, blowtorch, dehydrator, whipping siphon (freeze dryer/bincho-tan grill/centrifuge/homogenizer optional) | **Time** 24 h.

1. **Razor Clam "Takoyaki,"** with grey mullet bottarga, sea bean, Mutsu apple — clam cream set into a **low-acyl gellan + iota carrageenan** fluid gel, formed into spheres, breaded with an aerated **methylcellulose** foam double-dip, deep-fried.
2. **Shigoku Oyster** with fizzy grapes, fresh wasabi, miso powder — grapes carbonated via **N₂O/CO₂ siphon**; oyster-juice foam stabilized with sucrose esters (Sucro).
3. **Marinated Geoduck**, young ginger, shiso — siphon-sliced 2 mm thin.
4. **Spot Prawn and Lotus Root Tempura** with smoked white soy sauce — N₂O-siphon-aerated sake/vodka batter.
5. **Sea Urchin Tofu**, Tokyo negi, sesame, ponzu (inspired by Yoshihiro Murata) — dashi set with **gelatin + agar** into a sliceable custard.
6. **Pen Shell Clam**, Pluot, myoga, scallop mochi — clams dusted with freeze-dried scallop powder.
7. **Bincho-tan-grilled Sea Cucumber**, maitake mushroom, shisito pepper.
8. **Abalone and Foie Gras Shabu-Shabu** with yuba and enoki — broth **centrifuged at 27,500g for 1 h** to clarify.

**Standout technique:** the widest single-dish spread of hydrocolloid/spherification/centrifuge/carbonation techniques in the volume — 8 distinct Modernist methods across 8 courses.

**Plating:** each course individually composed; course 8 finishes as a tableside shabu-shabu with a warm tosazu dipping emulsion.

#### 6. Oyster Stew
*Milk skin, salsify, grilled avocado, lemon balm*

A Pacific Northwest deconstruction nodding to Thomas Keller's "Oysters and Pearls" — vinegar-marinated oyster escabeche with tapioca "pearls," poured over with a briny consommé and floated with savory milk-skin veils. **Yield** 4 | **Equipment** sous vide, blowtorch (vacuum filtration optional) | **Time** 3 h.

- **Oyster Escabeche** (12 pieces) — oysters poached sous vide **45°C/113°F for 10 min** (a gentle temperature preserving delicate texture), then marinated.
- **Oyster Consommé** (300 g) — multi-aromatic broth clarified via **vacuum filtration**.
- **Milk Skin (or Soy Yuba)** — floating veil garnish.
- **Salsify Fondant** (100 g) — sous vide 95°C/203°F 1 h, a butter-free "fondant" technique.
- **Pickled Tapioca Pearl** (200 g) — brined in oyster juice, echoing Keller's "pearls."
- **Avocado Purée** (adapted from Wylie Dufresne, 150 g) — stabilized with Ultra-Sperse 5 + ascorbic acid (anti-browning), torched in dots.

**Standout technique:** vacuum-filtration consommé clarification; Ultra-Sperse 5/ascorbic-acid-stabilized avocado purée.

**Plating:** cold salsify fondant, marinated oysters, and milk skin arranged on chilled plates with torched avocado dots and lemon balm; cold consommé poured tableside.

**Chapter-wide hydrocolloids/additives:** agar, locust bean gum, whey protein isolate, low-acyl gellan + iota carrageenan, methylcellulose, sucrose esters (Sucro), 160 Bloom gelatin, Ultra-Sperse 5, ascorbic acid, N-Zorbit M.

---

### CHAPTER 24 — EGGS

#### 1. The Breakfast Egg — *inspired by Alain Passard's L'Arpège egg*
*Fried egg foam, sweet spices, coffee butter*

Passard's iconic warm-water-bathed egg (spices, savory whipped cream, sherry vinegar, fleur de sel, maple syrup) is tipped toward classic American breakfast: cream is blended with fried (not raw) egg white into a whipped-siphon foam; smoked maple syrup stands in for bacon. **Yield** 4 | **Equipment** sous vide, whipping siphon (rotary grinder/Dremel optional) | **Time** 4 h 20 min.

- **Coffee Butter** (125 g) — whole coffee beans steamed with butter (or sous vide 90°C/195°F 4 h) to infuse the fat.
- **Fried Egg Foam** (220 g) — fried egg whites + gelatin-dispersed cream, loaded into an N₂O siphon.
- **Compressed Green Apple** (50 g) — vacuum-compressed with green apple vinegar.
- **Sweet Spice Blend** (25 g) — star anise, ginger, vanilla, black pepper, cinnamon, nutmeg.
- **Egg Yolk Confit** (80 g) — blended yolks **sous vide 67°C/153°F for 45 min** (fully pasteurizing); served in a cleaned, dried, reused eggshell.

**Standout technique:** precise sous vide yolk pasteurization at 67°C/153°F; turning fried egg white into a gelatin-stabilized siphon foam.

**Plating:** yolk confit piped into the reheated eggshell, seasoned, topped with compressed apple; fried egg foam dispensed to the shell's edge; finished with smoked maple syrup and flaky salt; brioche "soldiers" panfried in coffee butter alongside.

#### 2. Mushroom Omelet
*Mushroom marmalade, scrambled egg foam, fine herbs*

Uses sous vide's precise temperature control — the same principle behind the custard-yolk poached egg — to reinvent the omelet as a wafer-thin egg sheet filled with lightened scrambled eggs and mushroom marmalade. The authors call this "our favorite recipe in the book." **Yield** 4 | **Equipment** sous vide, combi oven or CVap, whipping siphon (pastry comb optional) | **Time** 2 h (10 h with unattended prep).

- **Scrambled Egg Foam** (200 g) — whole egg/yolk/butter/milk sous vide **72°C/162°F until pastry-cream texture** (~25 min), loaded into an N₂O siphon.
- **Mushroom Purée** (optional, ~130 g) — morel/porcini/shiitake/chanterelle; truffle concentrate can substitute.
- **Omelet Base** (300 g) — mushroom purée combed into thin lines with a **pastry comb** on a silicone mat, egg batter poured over 2 mm thick, steamed at **82°C/180°F, 100% humidity** (~3 min) — the comb lines run through the sheet as visible stripes.
- **Mushroom Marmalade** (35 g) — low-and-slow mushroom/shallot cooked in clarified butter and chicken jus, finished with chervil, chives, parsley, tarragon.

**Standout technique:** sous vide scrambled-egg custard aerated in a siphon; a pastry-comb technique striping mushroom purée through a steamed egg sheet for a through-and-through visual effect.

**Plating:** omelet base reheated in a combi oven; scrambled egg foam piped down the center, marmalade alongside, folded over, brushed with hot brown butter, garnished with chive blossoms.

#### 3. Oeufs en Meurette
*Constructed pinot noir, crispy eggshell, traditional accompaniments*

Reinterprets the Burgundian classic (eggs poached in red wine sauce with bacon, pearl onion, mushroom, fried bread) with a fully "constructed" faux-wine sauce built from fruit rather than actual wine, and a port/beet-juice bath that dyes the poached egg a mottled purple like Chinese tea eggs. **Yield** 4 | **Equipment** sous vide | **Time** 14 h.

- **Bacon Chips** (100 g) — bacon soaked in a maple/glucose/isomalt/sorbitol syrup, dehydrated **60°C/140°F for 12–14 h** until crisp.
- **Liquid Center Duck Egg** (340 g / 4 eggs) — boiled 4 min to fix the white, then sous vide **62°C/143°F for ~30 min** for a liquid-custard center, then soaked **12 h in reduced port-wine/beet juice** to dye the shell purple.
- **Preserved Cinnamon Cap Mushrooms** (50 g) — fried, then vacuum-sealed in the cooled frying oil.
- **Sous Vide Glazed Pearl Onions** (50 g) — sous vide 90°C/194°F 2.5 h.
- **Brioche Toast** (50 g) — dusted with albumin powder before frying for extra structure.
- **Constructed Red Wine Glaze** (65 g) — sous vide blackberry/blueberry purée sharpened with malic and tartaric acid, deepened with olive brine and shaved oak chips, balanced with **tannin powder (Obipektin Enocianin-A)** — a "wine" sauce built entirely without wine.

**Standout technique:** two-stage cook (boiling water bath, then 62°C sous vide) plus a 12-hour port/beet dye soak for the marbled "tea egg" shell effect; a fully reconstructed wine sauce using precise acid/tannin dosing instead of actual wine.

**Plating:** egg centered in the bowl, seasoned, garnished with mushrooms, bacon chips, glazed onions, chive, chervil; topped with brioche toast; constructed wine glaze poured over tableside.

**Chapter-wide hydrocolloids/additives:** 160 Bloom gelatin, albumin powder, tannin powder (Enocianin-A), malic acid, tartaric acid, isomalt, sorbitol.

---

### CHAPTER 25 — STARCHES

#### 1. Cocoa Tajarin
*Bitter cocoa, Pacific sea urchin, pink grapefruit*

Piedmont's hand-cut, high-egg-yolk tajarin noodle gets part of its flour replaced with cocoa powder — egg yolk and cocoa are both >25% fat by weight, and cocoa fat melts near body temperature, giving an extra melt-in-the-mouth quality. **Yield** 4 | **Equipment** sous vide | **Time** 13 h (90 days if making the optional sea urchin bottarga).

- **Sea Urchin Bottarga** (optional, 150 g) — sous vide 64°C/150°F 50 min, cured 12 h, then **air-dried 90 days and dipped 3× in 80°C/176°F beeswax** for a protective coating.
- **Pink Grapefruit Confit** (250 g) — sous vide 90°C/194°F 4 h in a fructose/isomalt/citric-acid syrup.
- **Cocoa Tajarin** (400 g) — 00 flour/semolina/cocoa powder/gluten/egg-yolk dough rested 12 h refrigerated, cut 2 mm, tossed warm in melted deodorized cocoa butter instead of dairy butter.
- **Sea Urchin Butter** (200 g) — sake/vermouth/shallot/tarragon reduction blended with sea urchin, grapefruit syrup, butter.

**Standout technique:** cocoa powder substituted for a portion of pasta flour; sidebar notes that salt, not sugar, is what actually masks bitterness — explaining the seasoning logic behind pairing bitter cocoa/grapefruit with salty sea urchin.

**Plating:** tajarin plated, sea urchin butter poured over, garnished with grapefruit "droplets," diced confit, black pepper, tarragon, sea urchin tongues, and grated bottarga.

#### 2. Spaghetti alle Vongole
*Bagna cauda, venus clam, young garlic*

Argues dried pasta is correct for this dish because only a firm al dente core (impossible with fresh pasta) gives the right bite; recommends bronze-die-extruded pasta for its rough, sauce-gripping surface. **Yield** 4 | **Equipment** sous vide (combi oven/centrifuge optional) | **Time** 40 h.

- **Clam Juice** (175 g) — steamed/boiled sous vide just until clams release juice (3 min).
- **Bagna Cauda Consommé** (200 g) — garlic/onion/clam-juice/anchovy broth **clarified via centrifuge at 27,500g for 1 h**.
- **Brown Butter-Poached Green Garlic** (60 g) — poached in clarified brown butter 1 h.
- **Walnut Clam Marmalade** (125 g).
- **Parsley Foam** (265 g) — parsley juice blended with **deoiled soy lecithin powder** for foaming.
- **Stuffed Clams** (12) — sous vide 70°C/158°F 15 min, stuffed and broiled; sidebar tip: adding chili to the purging water speeds sand expulsion via capsaicin irritation.

**Standout technique:** 27,500g centrifuge clarification of an anchovy-garlic broth; soy-lecithin-foamed parsley juice.

**Plating:** spaghetti topped with 3 broiled stuffed clams, poached garlic, walnut marmalade; parsley foamed tableside; consommé poured at the table.

#### 3. Russian Pelmeni
*Heather-smoked sturgeon, Kusshi oysters, golden beets, poppy seeds*

Traces pelmeni's likely Chinese origin (kinship with potstickers) via Mongol transmission to Tatarstan/Siberia. A buckwheat dough strengthened with **Activa** wraps a heather-smoked sturgeon rillette; the same dough alternately becomes noodles in a borscht-style broth. **Yield** 4 | **Equipment** smoker, sous vide | **Time** 13 h.

- **Buckwheat Dough** (525 g) — buckwheat + AP flour + **Activa TI/RM (6%)** to firm the low-gluten dough, rested 12 h refrigerated (celiac sufferers should avoid Activa-bound doughs, per sidebar).
- **Heather-Smoked Sturgeon** (450 g) — cured 8 h, **cold-smoked with dried heather blossoms and cherrywood at 52°C/125°F, 50% RH, 4 h**.
- **Heather-Smoked Sturgeon Rillettes** (200 g) — thickened/stabilized with **Ultra-Sperse 3**.
- **Sous Vide Golden Beets** (225 g) — sous vide 88°C/190°F 1 h.
- **Borscht Broth** (optional, 400 g) — beet/rhubarb juice, fish stock, caraway, sous vide 85°C/185°F 30 min.
- **Beet Juice-Fed Oysters** (optional) — oysters fed beet juice to turn a "stunning red."
- **Oyster Butter Emulsion** (225 g) — Darjeeling-tea-infused reduction emulsified with butter and crème fraîche.
- **Buckwheat Pelmeni** (565 g, 12 dumplings) — traditional "ear" fold around a finger.

**Standout technique:** Activa-firmed low-gluten buckwheat dough; naturally beet-juice-dyed oysters.

**Plating:** pelmeni boiled, tossed in warm brown butter with poppy seeds; plated with oyster bellies, sliced beets, cucumber; hot oyster butter and borscht broth poured tableside.

#### 4. Paella Valenciana
*Rabbit, langoustine, garden snail, horchata emulsion, soccarat tuile*

Traces paella to Valencian field workers cooking over burning grapevine trimmings; snails are cooked sous vide until meltingly tender, and the rice is smoked to recreate the aroma of burning vines. **Yield** 4 | **Equipment** none required (sous vide/freeze dryer optional) | **Time** 13 h (24 h with optional Smoked Bomba Rice).

- **Paella Stock** (750 g) — rabbit trimmings and vegetables, **pressure-cooked at 1 bar/15 psi for 1 h**.
- **Paella Rice** (350 g) — bomba rice **pressure-cooked 3 min at 1 bar/15 psi**, then rapidly chilled to halt cooking — a parcooking technique; sidebar notes bomba rice swells 3× dry volume in width, not length, staying firm without stickiness.
- **Sous Vide Rabbit Loin** (450 g) — two loins **Activa-bonded** into a uniform cylinder, sous vide 57°C/135°F to core 56°C/133°F.
- **Sous Vide Braised Snails** (400 g) — sous vide 68°C/154°F 5 h.
- **Sofrito** (150 g) — long, classic reduction.
- **Sous Vide Artichoke** (100 g) — sous vide 90°C/194°F 45 min with ascorbic acid.
- **Horchata Foam** (200 g) — chufa (tigernut) milk foamed with **deoiled soy lecithin and whey protein isolate**.
- **Seared Langoustine Tails** (160 g).
- **Soccarat Tuile** (170 g, ~24 discs) — sous vide-cooked bomba rice puréed, spread thin, sprinkled with puffed wild rice, baked crisp — an edible replica of paella's prized scorched-rice crust.

**Standout technique:** pressure cooking for both stock extraction and rapid rice parcooking; Activa-bonded rabbit-loin cylinders; soy-lecithin/whey-protein horchata foam.

**Plating:** rice topped with sliced rabbit, snails, artichoke, langoustine; garnished with horchata foam and a soccarat tuile shard.

#### 5. Astronaut Ramen
*Pork and young ginger*

Tells the history of Momofuku Ando's instant ramen and his proudest achievement — vacuum-packed "Space Ramen" for the space shuttle Discovery — via an entirely **freeze-dried** dish where even the serving cup dissolves into the broth. **Yield** 4 | **Equipment** freeze dryer, sous vide | **Time** 22 h.

- **"Styrofoam" Lid and Cup** (2 g / 8 g) — 160 Bloom gelatin + xanthan gum spread thin, molded, **freeze-dried 12 h** — dissolves completely into the hot broth, functioning as a hidden thickener ("the cup and lid become thickeners that give the broth body").
- **Ramen Noodles** (14.5 g) — high-gluten dough, frozen, **freeze-dried 12 h** — the added gluten lets these taste al dente even freeze-dried, unlike most fresh pastas.
- **Freeze-Dried Egg Yolk** (9 g) — sous vide 67°C/153°F 45 min, rolled, punched into discs, freeze-dried.
- **Freeze-Dried Pork** (70 g) — Mangalitsa belly sous vide 82°C/180°F 8 h; Mangalitsa shoulder sous vide 88°C/190°F 8 h, both freeze-dried.
- **Ramen Vegetables and Stock** — freeze-dried mushroom/bamboo/ginger/carrot/scallion/lotus; stock freeze-dried into a powder sprinkled tableside.

**Standout technique:** the entire dish is a freeze-drying showcase, including an edible, xanthan-reinforced gelatin "Styrofoam" serving vessel engineered to dissolve on contact with hot water.

**Plating:** freeze-dried components layered inside the "Styrofoam" cup, powder sieved on top, lid placed on; 90 g of 70°C/158°F water poured over at the table dissolves the packaging entirely, leaving a bowl of ramen.

#### 6. Shanghai Soup Dumplings
*Pork and crab juice, toasted sesame seeds, scallion marmalade*

Traces xiao long bao to Nanxiang outside Shanghai; the dumpling's gelled-stock-in-dough tradition is reinvented by **encasing the soup directly in a spherified gel skin** instead of dough — reverse spherification via calcium lactate inside, sodium alginate outside. **Yield** 4 | **Equipment** pressure cooker | **Time** 4 h.

- **Triple Dungeness Crab and Pork Stock Infusion** (850 g) — crab, chicken wings, and pork **pressure-cooked at 1 bar/15 psi for 1 h**, briefly spice-infused.
- **Crab and Ginger Marble** (20 g / 20 marbles) — steamed crab bound with **Activa RM**, rested 4+ h.
- **Herb-Embedded Pasta Veil** — a translucent noodle sheet replacing the traditional wrapper.
- **Crab and Pork Stock Spheres** (240 g / 24 spheres) — cold stock dosed with **calcium lactate (3%)**, poured into hemisphere molds around a crab marble, **frozen solid**, then dropped into a heated **sodium alginate bath (0.5%, 60–65°C/140–149°F)** for 2 min as the frozen sphere thaws and the alginate reacts on contact — explicit **reverse spherification**, traced from 1942 artificial cherries through Ferran Adrià's 2003 eiBulli pea-purée spheres.

**Standout technique:** reverse spherification encasing liquid soup and a crab marble in an edible, transparent membrane, entirely replacing the dumpling's dough wrapper.

**Plating:** scallion marmalade on porcelain spoons, one warmed sphere on top, draped with the herb-embedded pasta veil, seasoned with a soy-vinegar infusion, garnished with chive and young ginger.

**Chapter-wide hydrocolloids/additives:** isomalt, xanthan gum, Ultra-Sperse 3, Activa RM/TI, 160 Bloom gelatin, sodium alginate + calcium lactate, deoiled soy lecithin, whey protein isolate.

---

### CHAPTER 26 — FRUITS AND VEGETABLES

#### 1. Onion Tart — *inspired by Pierre Hermé*
*Gruyère custard, onion arlette, onion gratin*

An "upside-down" reinvention (gratin on the bottom, onion sablé "crust" on top), inspired by Hermé's savory-sweet pairings; the gratin technique itself is drawn from Alain Passard's slow, never-caramelized onion cooking. **Yield** 4 | **Equipment** sous vide, meat slicer (freeze dryer optional) | **Time** 3 h (12 h with homemade onion powder).

- **Onion Arlette** (500 g) — puff pastry layered with caramelized-onion sugar, rolled, sliced paper-thin on a meat slicer, baked translucent.
- **Onion Sablé** (350 g) — onion-infused butter, almond powder, isomalt, sous vide egg yolk, freeze-dried onion powder.
- **Glazed Pearl Onion** (230 g) — sous vide 85°C/185°F.
- **Gruyère Custard** (670 g) — onion stock + Gruyère set with **sodium citrate + iota carrageenan (0.5%) + kappa carrageenan (0.2%)**, cast and cubed.
- **Onion Gratin** (inspired by Alain Passard, 250 g) — slow-sautéed onion deglazed with Gewürztraminer until fully evaporated.

**Standout technique:** dual-carrageenan/sodium-citrate custard engineered for a sliceable, stable set; meat-slicer lamination for the arlette.

**Plating:** broiled gratin centered on the plate, topped with warmed custard cubes and a sablé rectangle; garnished with seared pearl onion, arlette pieces, chive blossom, lime zest.

#### 2. Lentil Salad
*Foie gras parfait, pickled Bing cherry, hazelnut nougatine, chamomile*

Reframes French green lentils (lentilles vertes du Puy) as a delicate, creamy canvas; foie gras parfait is dipped in cherry gelée to resemble an actual cherry (nodding to Paul Liebrandt) — "literally the cherry on top." **Yield** 4 | **Equipment** sous vide, blowtorch, silicone hemisphere molds, vacuum reduction equipment (LN₂ optional) | **Time** 11 h.

- **Foie Gras Parfait** (600 g) — sous vide 57°C/135°F to core 56°C/133°F, piped into hemispheres, joined into spheres, frozen.
- **Foie Gras Cherries** (150 g) — sour cherry juice set with **low-acyl gellan (Kelcogel F)** into a fluid gel + xanthan gum (0.4%); frozen foie gras spheres dipped to resemble real cherries.
- **Pickled Bing Cherry** (650 g) — vacuum-infused with vinegar/sour cherry brine.
- **Dried Cherry and Hazelnut Nougatine** (240 g) — sugar/trehalose/glucose cooked to 170°C, rolled thin, shattered.
- **Cherry Vinaigrette** (400 g) — emulsified with xanthan gum (0.44%) and a trace of chamomile essential oil.
- **Sherry Gel Cube** (215 g) — chamomile-steeped water set with **agar (1.3%)**.
- **Sous Vide Lentils** (650 g) — sous vide 90°C/194°F with an aromatic sachet; sidebar notes hard/alkaline water affects lentil texture, hence distilled water.

**Standout technique:** a gellan fluid-gel "faux cherry" coating over a frozen foie gras sphere — a molecular trompe-l'œil.

**Plating:** lentils dressed and folded with pickled cherry, sherry gel cubes, hazelnut, black radish, chervil; topped with one foie gras cherry per bowl; finished with chamomile blossom, frisée, nougatine shards.

#### 3. Sweet Pea Fricassee
*Crispy goat's milk, young ginger, cinnamon, sweet pea velouté*

Reworks the classic French fricassee into a light, vegetarian spring dish — fresh peas warmed only just above 70°C/158°F to preserve their "garden" flavor before it turns "cooked." **Yield** 4 | **Equipment** dehydrator (centrifuge optional) | **Time** 2 h.

- **Crispy Goat's Milk Ricotta Dumpling** (adapted from Wylie Dufresne, 290 g) — thickened with **Ultra-Tex 4**, dehydrated then deep-fried.
- **Cinnamon-Scented Pea Juice** (210 g) — **centrifuged at 27,500g for 1 h** for clarity.
- **Sweet Pea and Young Ginger Fricassee** (90 g) — sous vide-style gentle cook at **70°C/158°F for 18 min**.
- **Pea Vine Salad** (200 g) — vinaigrette thickened with microcrystalline cellulose (optional).

**Standout technique:** centrifuge clarification for a crystal-clear pea velouté; Ultra-Tex 4 for a moldable-but-crispy dumpling.

**Plating:** fried dumplings alongside the warmed fricassee, topped with pea vine salad, pea blossom, tendrils, ginger matchsticks; cinnamon pea juice poured tableside.

#### 4. Strawberry Gazpacho — *inspired by David Kinch*
*Green strawberry, piquillo pepper, oxalis, macadamia nut oil*

Traces gazpacho to the pre-tomato Moorish *ajo blanco* through David Kinch's (Manresa) strawberry variant; adds a sour rhubarb sorbet (chef Sam Fahey-Burke) for the classic rhubarb-strawberry pairing. **Yield** 4 | **Equipment** Pacojet or ice cream maker (centrifuge/freeze dryer optional) | **Time** 13 h.

- **Strawberry Consommé** (265 g) — **centrifuged at 27,500g** for clarity.
- **Strawberry Gazpacho** (850 g) — macerated 12 h, blended, sieved; sidebar cites Heston Blumenthal's trick of adding a splash of fresh-juiced fruit at the last minute to "regenerate" lost aromatics via residual enzymes.
- **Piquillo Pepper and Strawberry Salad** (70 g).
- **Seared Strawberry** (100 g) — dusted with freeze-dried strawberry powder, seared hard.
- **Sour Rhubarb Sorbet** (780 g) — sous vide 90°C/194°F, Pacojet-spun.

**Standout technique:** double centrifuge clarification; the Blumenthal fresh-juice "enzyme regeneration" trick as a make-ahead flavor fix.

**Plating:** seared strawberry at the bowl's base, topped with piquillo-strawberry salad and rhubarb sorbet; garnished with macadamia, oxalis, green strawberry slices; chilled gazpacho poured tableside.

#### 5. Crispy Cauliflower
*Marcona almond, Anjou pear, chaat masala, tamarind pudding*

Pivots from Levantine fried-cauliflower traditions to an Indian chaat-masala profile after tasting chef Anjana Shanker's spice blend "where no single spice stands out"; cauliflower's low sugar content lets it fry a long time without scorching, for a deep, nutty crust. **Yield** 4 | **Equipment** sous vide, deep fryer, whipping siphon (colloid mill optional) | **Time** 3 h.

- **Chaat Masala** (70 g) — roasted coriander/cumin/ajowan/chili with amchoor and anardana.
- **Tamarind Purée** (240 g) — sous vide 90°C/194°F, thickened with xanthan gum (0.2%).
- **Marcona Almond Butter Paste** (350 g) — colloid-milled, thickened with Ultra-Sperse 5.
- **Cauliflower Foam** (450 g) — "cauliflower milk" gelled with **low-acyl gellan + locust bean gum**, blended with browned cauliflower purée and loaded into a whipping siphon for a *warm* foam.
- **Preserved Pear in Mustard Oil** (300 g) — melon-balled pear vacuum-sealed in mustard-oil/curry-leaf syrup.
- **Deep-Fried Cauliflower** (450 g) — cut into steaks and florets, fried **195°C/385°F** until very dark/crispy.

**Standout technique:** a gellan/locust-bean-gum gel base whipped into a warm siphon foam (unusual — most siphon foams are cold); colloid-milled nut butter.

**Plating:** fried cauliflower steak centered on a warmed plate, dotted with almond butter and tamarind purée; pear spheres and fried florets scattered around; warm foam and chaat masala dusting to finish.

#### 6. Watermelon Bulgogi — *inspired by Andoni Luis Aduriz*
*Kimchi, ssamjang paste, perilla*

A "faux meat" bulgogi crediting Aduriz (Mugaritz) as the first to apply vacuum compression as a sous vide technique — here turning porous watermelon flesh into a dense, chewy, deep-red "meat" via partial dehydration. **Yield** 4 | **Equipment** sous vide, dehydrator | **Time** 13 h.

- **Watermelon Meat** — compressed/dehydrated over ~13 h to a chewy, "rare-meat"-like texture.
- **Watermelon Rind Kimchi** (140 g) — brined with scallion, ginger, garlic, **lactic acid**, dried shrimp, chili.
- **Bulgogi Glaze** (~525 g) — classic soy/sugar/sesame oil/ginger/garlic reduction.
- **Ssamjang Paste** (275 g) — kochujang + doenjang, rice wine, sesame oil, garlic, Fresno chili.

**Standout technique:** vacuum compression + dehydration transforming watermelon's cellular structure into a dense, meat-like texture, explicitly credited to Aduriz as a sous vide innovation.

**Plating:** dehydrated watermelon "meat" seared hard, brushed with warm bulgogi glaze; ssamjang spooned on plates; topped with watermelon slices and rind kimchi; served with perilla and lettuce leaves for wrapping, ssam-style.

**Chapter-wide hydrocolloids/additives:** sodium citrate, iota + kappa carrageenan, isomalt, low-acyl gellan (×2), xanthan gum (×3), agar, locust bean gum, Ultra-Tex 4, Ultra-Sperse 5, microcrystalline cellulose (Avicel), lactic acid.

---

## 4. Cross-Volume Technique Index (Highlights)

A few techniques recur across the whole volume and mark it as a signature Modernist Cuisine collection:

- **Centrifugation (typically 27,500g)** for clarifying stocks, juices, and sauces — used in at least 9 dishes across Tender Cuts, Tough Cuts, Shellfish, Starches, and Fruits/Vegetables.
- **Activa (transglutaminase) bonding** — welding skin back onto meat (Crispy Hay-Smoked Chicken, Pigeon en Salmis), bonding two cuts into one uniform portion (Rare Flatiron Steak, Skate pavé, Paella rabbit loin, Tuna/escolar checkerboard), and firming low-gluten doughs (Russian Pelmeni).
- **Spherification** (standard and reverse) — Skate's caper "droplets," Shanghai Soup Dumplings' reverse-spherified soup spheres.
- **Liquid nitrogen** — cryosearing (Autumn Harvest Pork Roast, Crispy Hay-Smoked Chicken), cryopoaching a meringue (Salmon Rus), and flash-freezing produce for clean cellular separation (Thai Crab Miang's pomelo, Pulpo a la Gallega's chorizo).
- **Freeze-drying** — an entire dish built around it (Astronaut Ramen), plus garnishes throughout (onion powder, scallop powder, corn, strawberry powder).
- **Pressure cooking (commonly 1 bar/15 psi gauge)** for fast stock extraction and for forcing reactions (Maillard-browning crème fraîche in Rack of Lamb, per-grain texture control in Pigeon en Salmis).
- **Precision sous vide egg cookery** — 67°C/153°F yolk pasteurization (Breakfast Egg, Astronaut Ramen), 72°C/162°F scrambled-egg custard (Mushroom Omelet), 62°C/143°F duck-egg liquid center (Oeufs en Meurette).
- **Whipping-siphon aeration** used for both cold and warm foams (Suet Mousseline, Cauliflower Foam), tempura batters (Fish and Chips, Shellfish Omakase), and even fried "beignet" solids of what would classically be a sauce (Pot-au-Feu's ravigote beignet).

---

A small number of dish title/headnote blocks (Braised Short Ribs, Osso Buco Milanese, American BBQ in Chapter 20; Black Cod "Fredy Girardet," Shrimp Cocktail, and Lobster Américaine's opening spread in Chapters 22–23) sit on image-heavy title spreads that did not extract as plain text via `pdftotext`; their full component lists, techniques, and (where present) plating notes were recovered and are included above.*

# Modernist Cuisine — Volume 6: Kitchen Manual
### Nathan Myhrvold, with Chris Young and Maxime Bilet (The Cooking Lab, 2011)

Condensed technical reference synthesized from the source PDF (376 pages, first edition ISBN 978-0-9827610-0-7). Volume 6 is a compressed, chapter-numbered companion to Volumes 2–5 of the *Modernist Cuisine* set — chapter numbers here match the corresponding chapters in the full volumes (e.g., page references of the form "2-296" refer to Volume 2, page 296), and selected component recipes from Volume 5's plated-dish recipes are reprinted in whichever chapter is most relevant. This file captures the full structural table of contents plus a comprehensive synthesis of the book's actual reference content — the "Best Bets" parametric tables (ingredient/method/temperature/time matrices), sous vide pasteurization and doneness tables, curing/brining ratios, thickener and gel dosages, emulsion and foam technique parameters, and the back-of-book conversion/composition tables — meant as a usable kitchen-science reference, not a narrative summary.

### Nathan Myhrvold & Francisco Migoya (The Cooking Lab)

Condensed technical reference synthesized from the source PDF (414 pages).
