<!-- ===== INÍCIO: the-science-of-spice.md ===== -->

# The Science of Spice — Dr. Stuart Farrimond

**Título completo:** *The Science of Spice: Understand Flavour Connections and Revolutionize Your Cooking*
**Autor principal:** Dr. Stuart Farrimond (com contribuições de Bernard Lahousse e Peter Coucquyt, da Foodpairing)
**Editora:** DK (Dorling Kindersley) / Penguin Random House, 1ª edição, 2018 — ISBN13 9780241302149
**Arquivo:** "The science of spice ... Anna's Archive.pdf" (226 páginas)

---

## 1. Visão estrutural

O livro se divide em três blocos principais, sinalizados por lombadas coloridas:

### Bloco 1 — SPICE SCIENCE (p. 8–17)
Fundamentos de ciência do sabor aplicados a especiarias:
- **What is a Spice?** (p.10-11) — taxonomia botânica das especiarias por parte da planta usada: caules (canela, cássia), raízes/rizomas (cúrcuma, gengibre, alho, açafrão-da-índia), frutos (pimenta-do-reino, sumagre), sementes (cominho, cardamomo, anis-estrelado), flores (cravo, açafrão). Cada categoria é explicada pela função biológica do composto de sabor na planta (comunicação, defesa contra predadores, atração de polinizadores).
- **Spices and their Flavour Compounds** (p.12-13) — define as **12 famílias de sabor** usadas no livro inteiro como sistema organizador (ver seção 2).
- **Periodic Table of Spices** (p.14-15) — uma "tabela periódica" visual com todas as ~50 especiarias do livro, cada uma com um símbolo de 2 letras (ex.: Ci=Cinnamon, Ca=Cassia, Cl=Clove, Al=Allspice, An=Anise, St=Star Anise, Pe=Pepper, Ch=Chilli, Cm=Cardamom, Gi=Ginger, Sa=Saffron, Tu=Turmeric, Ba=Bay...), coloridas por família de sabor.
- **Creating Spice Pairings and Blends** (p.16-17) — método passo a passo (4 etapas) para criar misturas de especiarias: (1) escolher a(s) família(s) de sabor principal(is), (2) checar a "ciência de blend" de cada especiaria candidata, (3) escolher as especiarias primárias, (4) adicionar complexidade cruzando compostos secundários compartilhados. Exemplo dado no livro: allspice (eugenol) + star anise (anethole) — mesma família, aroma complementar — depois enriquecidos com galangal (cineole) e coentro (linalol).

### Bloco 2 — WORLD OF SPICE (p. 18–77)
Atlas de especiarias por região, com mapas de rotas comerciais históricas. Sete regiões, cada uma subdividida em países/sub-regiões com um cartão "Spice Palette" (Signature / Supporting / Supplementary) e uma receita de blend local:

| Região | Subdivisões (países) | Página |
|---|---|---|
| Oriente Médio | Síria, Turquia, Israel, Líbano, Iraque, Irã, Egito, Península Arábica | 20–29 |
| África | Chifre da África, Magrebe, Leste, Oeste, Central, Sul da África | 30–37 |
| Sul da Ásia | Índia, Sri Lanka, etc. | 38–45 |
| Sudeste Asiático | Tailândia, Vietnã, Myanmar, etc. | 46–53 |
| Leste Asiático | China, Japão, etc. | 54–61 |
| Américas | América do Norte, Central, do Sul, Caribe | 62–69 |
| Europa | Mediterrâneo, Europa central, etc. | 70–77 |

Exemplos de blends locais documentados com receita completa: *Za'atar* (Síria), *Baharat turco* (Turquia), *Zhug* e *Taklia* (Líbano), *Baharat árabe* (Iraque), *Advieh* (Irã), *Dukkah* (Egito), *Hawaij* (Península Arábica/Iêmen), *Niter kibbeh* (Chifre da África), *Harissa* (Magrebe).

### Bloco 3 — SPICE PROFILES (p. 78–209)
O núcleo do livro: fichas individuais por especiaria, agrupadas nas **12 famílias de sabor** (cada uma com sua faixa de páginas e cor):

1. Sweet Warming Phenols (p.80) — canela, cássia, cravo, allspice, anis, anis-estrelado, baunilha, alcaçuz, erva-doce...
2. Warming Terpenes (p.102) — noz-moscada, macis, grãos-do-paraíso, annatto...
3. Fragrant Terpenes (p.116) — coentro, cardamomo...
4. Earthy Terpenes (p.126) — cominho, nigela...
5. Penetrating Terpenes (p.130) — galangal, cardamomo preto, grãos de Selim, fenacho...
6. Citrus Terpenes (p.142) — capim-limão, lima seca, mirtilo-limão...
7. Sweet-Sour Acids (p.148) — tamarindo, amchoor, sumagre...
8. Fruity Aldehydes (p.160) — barberry, amora...
9. Toasty Pyrazines (p.164) — especiarias torradas/tostadas
10. Sulphurous Compounds (p.172) — alho, mostarda, asafétida...
11. Pungent Compounds (p.180) — pimenta-do-reino, pimenta malagueta, páprica, pimenta-de-sichuan...
12. Unique Compounds (p.194) — açafrão, cúrcuma, gengibre...

Cada ficha de especiaria segue o **mesmo template de 3 páginas**:
- **Página 1** (perfil): nome botânico, sinônimos, composto de sabor principal, partes usadas, método de cultivo, preparo comercial, usos não-culinários, ilustração botânica, formas de compra (em pó vs. inteiro), mapa de região de cultivo e "Spice Story" (história/trivia).
- **Página 2** ("Blending Science"): diagrama em árvore mostrando o composto de sabor principal e os compostos secundários, com sugestões de parceiros de especiaria ligados por cada composto compartilhado; caixa "Food Partners" (parceiros de ingrediente/prato); caixa "Blends to Try" (remissão às receitas de blend do livro); caixa "Release the Flavour" (técnica de cocção: quando adicionar, se precisa de gordura/álcool/água/calor para liberar o sabor).
- Ocasionalmente uma terceira página com receita completa ilustrando o uso da especiaria (11 receitas ao todo, listadas no sumário: salmão chinês a vapor com anis-estrelado, frango e berinjela biryani, ejjeh com curry de amendoim, salada laab, granita de tâmara e tamarindo, sorvete de gergelim preto, rosetas de maçã, vieiras com açafrão, adobo filipino, etc.)

O livro fecha com **World of Spice Further Recipes** (p.210), **Table of Spices and their Flavour Compounds** — uma tabela de referência cruzada de todos os compostos (p.214), Índice, e créditos.

## 2. Síntese do conteúdo central

A tese do livro: **especiarias combinam bem quando compartilham compostos de sabor (moléculas voláteis)**, e o autor construiu um sistema — a "Tabela Periódica de Especiarias" — agrupando ~50 especiarias em 12 famílias conforme o composto dominante:

| Família | Caráter | Compostos-exemplo | Especiarias-exemplo |
|---|---|---|---|
| Sweet Warming Phenols | quente, doce-aromático, às vezes amargo | eugenol (cravo), anetol (erva-doce) | canela, cássia, cravo, allspice, anis, baunilha |
| Warming Terpenes | quente sem doçura marcante, amadeirado/apimentado | sabineno (noz-moscada), germacreno | noz-moscada, macis, annatto |
| Fragrant Terpenes | fresco, pinho/floral | pineno (coentro), linalol | coentro |
| Earthy Terpenes | terroso, empoeirado, amadeirado | cuminaldeído (cominho), cimeno | cominho, nigela |
| Penetrating Terpenes | canforáceo/eucalipto, persistente | fenchona, cineole | galangal, cardamomo |
| Citrus Terpenes | cítrico, floral-herbáceo | citronelal, citral | capim-limão, lemon myrtle |
| Sweet-Sour Acids | azedo com doçura de fundo | ácidos hexanoico/pentanoico, cítrico | tamarindo, amchoor |
| Fruity Aldehydes | frutado, malte, verde | nonanal, hexanal | barberry, sumagre |
| Toasty Pyrazines | tostado, amanteigado, defumado | pirazinas (variam por especiaria) | especiarias torradas em geral |
| Sulphurous Compounds | cebola/repolho/rábano, pungente | isotiocianato (mostarda), dissulfeto de dialila (alho) | mostarda, alho, asafétida |
| Pungent Compounds | ardência pura, não é sabor | capsaicina (pimenta), piperina (pimenta-do-reino) | pimenta-do-reino, chili |
| Unique Compounds | terroso/penetrante/único | picrocrocina/safranal (açafrão), turmerona (cúrcuma) | açafrão, cúrcuma, gengibre |

**Método de blend proposto:** escolher especiarias da mesma família para uma base coerente, depois adicionar uma segunda especiaria de família diferente que compartilhe um composto secundário — construindo complexidade de forma deliberada em vez de por tentativa e erro. O livro reforça repetidamente o princípio de que "a complexidade aumenta o prazer" (mais compostos e sensações = prato mais saboroso), citado como achado de pesquisa sensorial.

**Componente cultural/geográfico:** a seção "World of Spice" ancora a ciência em tradições reais — cada região tem uma "assinatura de sabor" (ex.: Turquia = Fresh|Mild|Smoky; Irã = Floral|Musky|Sour; Magrebe = Earthy|Rich|Mild) e um blend característico documentado com receita e proporções exatas.

## 3. Amostra organizada de dados de especiarias e combinações

### Ficha completa: Canela (Cinnamon) — Sweet Warming Phenols
- **Composto principal:** cinamaldeído (aquece, não é doce por si, mas realça a percepção de doçura em outros ingredientes).
- **Compostos secundários:** cariofileno (amadeirado), eugenol (medicinal/quente), linalol (floral).
- **Parceiros sugeridos:** allspice e pimenta-do-reino (via cariofileno/eugenol), cominho (via cinamaldeído, aprofunda com calor terroso), cardamomo (via linalol, traz notas cítricas/florais complexas), cravo e anis/anis-estrelado (via eugenol, reforçam doçura e notas de alcaçuz).
- **Parceiros de prato:** frutas (pêssego, figo, maçã, pera antes de assar), massas doces (pain d'épices francês, panforte italiano), molho de tomate com berinjela assada, carnes vermelhas (tagine de cordeiro, beef stew iraniano *khorak*, pho vietnamita), pombo/pastilla marroquina.
- **Liberação do sabor:** adicionar cedo na cocção; gordura e álcool dispersam o cinamaldeído; vapor também funciona bem.

### Ficha completa: Cássia (Cassia)
- Também dominada por cinamaldeído, mas com taninos (adstringência) e cumarina (ausente na canela "verdadeira").
- **Parceiros:** carob, cominho, anis-estrelado, gengibre (compostos terrosos/amargos compartilhados); mahleb, noz-moscada, baunilha, anis (perfil doce mais amplo via cumarina); cardamomo, folha-de-louro, allspice, grãos de Selim (via cineol/eucalipto).
- **Alerta de segurança citado no livro:** cumarina em excesso pode causar dano hepático temporário; limite semanal recomendado citado: crianças 3,5g/semana, adultos 7g/semana — por isso prefira canela "verdadeira" para consumo regular.

### Ficha completa: Cravo (Clove)
- **Composto principal:** eugenol (o mais alto teor entre as especiarias do livro).
- **Parceiros:** allspice e alcaçuz (eugenol), grãos-do-paraíso, pimenta-do-reino e cacau (cariofileno).
- **Parceiros de prato:** tomate e repolho roxo, pêssegos em calda com canela/gengibre/cravo, carnes de porco/boi (pot-au-feu, curry de boi kerala), leite fervido para molho branco/kheer/payasam, bebidas quentes (chá, vinho quente).

### Ficha completa: Allspice (Pimenta-da-jamaica)
- **Composto principal:** eugenol; secundários cineol, linalol, felandreno.
- **Parceiros:** noz-moscada e canela (eugenol), galangal e cardamomo (cineol), anis-estrelado e pimenta-do-reino (felandreno), coentro e grãos de Selim (linalol).
- **Uso:** peixe cru com mostarda (escabeche mexicano), legumes doces (beterraba, batata-doce), carnes vermelhas, frutas com caroço, biscoitos e bolos.

### Ficha completa: Anis e Anis-estrelado
- **Anis:** composto principal anetol (13x mais doce que o açúcar), com anisyl álcool (cereja/baunilha), estragol e pineno.
- **Anis-estrelado:** também dominado por anetol, mas com perfil mais complexo (linalol floral); contém ácido chiquímico, usado na síntese do antiviral Tamiflu — mencionado como causa de escassez global durante epidemias de gripe.
- **Parceiros do anis-estrelado:** cardamomo e baunilha (cineol/eugenol), cravo e canela (compostos quentes compartilhados), pimenta-do-reino e dill (felandreno/cítrico).
- **Receita destacada:** *Chinese Steamed Salmon with Chilli and Star Anise* (salmão levemente defumado, anis-estrelado, gengibre, malagueta, molho de soja claro e escuro, óleo de gergelim/chili) — receita completa reproduzida no livro (p.92).

### Blends regionais documentados (receita + proporções)
- **Za'atar (Síria):** cominho, sal, gergelim, orégano seco, sumagre.
- **Baharat turco:** pimenta-do-reino, cominho, coentro, cravo, cardamomo, noz-moscada, canela, hortelã seca.
- **Zhug (Israel):** cardamomo, coentro, cominho, malagueta verde, alho, coentro fresco, salsa, azeite, limão.
- **Arabic baharat (Iraque):** pimenta-do-reino, allspice, cravo, coentro, cominho, canela, noz-moscada.
- **Advieh (Irã):** pétalas de rosa, cardamomo, cominho, canela, gengibre.
- **Dukkah (Egito):** avelã, amêndoa, coentro, cominho, gergelim, sal.
- **Hawaij (Iêmen):** pimenta-do-reino, cominho, cardamomo, coentro, cúrcuma (versão doce substitui pimenta/cominho por gengibre, cravo, canela, erva-doce).
- **Niter kibbeh (Chifre da África):** manteiga clarificada com cebola, alho, gengibre, fenacho, cominho, korarima/cardamomo, cúrcuma, orégano, manjericão, sálvia.
- **Harissa (Magrebe):** pimenta seca (bakhlouti), alho, sal, suco de limão, cominho, coentro, alcaravia, azeite.

## 4. Observações finais

Diferente do "Dicionário de Sabores" (que é enciclopédico e cultural), este livro é mais **sistemático e pedagógico**: propõe um framework replicável (as 12 famílias + tabela periódica) para que o leitor construa suas próprias misturas de especiarias com embasamento em química de sabor, complementado por um atlas cultural de blends regionais autênticos com receitas e proporções exatas. É especialmente útil como referência para formulação de temperos e blends, mais do que para consulta de pares ingrediente-a-ingrediente.

<!-- ===== FIM: the-science-of-spice.md ===== -->

<!-- ===== INÍCIO: zero-alinea.md ===== -->

# ZERO: A New Approach to Non-Alcoholic Drinks

**Authors:** Grant Achatz, Allen Hemberger, Nick Kokonas (with Micah Melton) — The Alinea Group
**Published:** First edition, Chicago, 2020. ISBN-13: 978-1-7330088-1-5
**Publisher:** The Alinea Group, LLC. Design & photography by Sarah & Allen Hemberger (Small Batch Creative).

This is the non-alcoholic beverage book from the team behind Alinea, Next, The Aviary, The Office, and Roister — Chicago's flagship fine-dining/cocktail restaurant group. It is not a "mocktail" book in the syrupy sense; it applies the group's culinary R&D methodology (the same one used for Alinea's tasting menus and The Aviary's cocktail program) to drinks that contain zero alcohol. Recipes originated in restaurant service but were rewritten/tested for home kitchens.

---

## 1. Structural Overview

The book is ~250 pages, organized into 5 chapters plus front matter (Introductions, "A Culinary Approach to Beverage Design," "Before We Begin," "Basic Syrups"):

| Chapter | Page range | Content |
|---|---|---|
| Mise en Place | 6–24 | Philosophy essay + equipment + basic syrups |
| Ch.1 — Back Bar | 25–44 | 14 non-alcoholic "spirit" building blocks (in the style of Gin, Whiskey, Tequila, Rum, Mezcal, vermouths, amari, bitters, liqueurs) |
| Ch.2 — Classic Cocktails | 45–64 | Non-alcoholic riffs on Mimosa, Paloma, Daiquiri, Jungle Bird, Margarita, Absinthe drip, French 75, Whiskey Sour, Black Manhattan, Amaro & Coke, Bijou, Negroni |
| Ch.3 — Modern Cocktails | 65–165 | ~30 original, restaurant-service drinks (Alinea, Next, The Aviary, The Office, Roister, St. Clair Supper Club) |
| Ch.4 — Wines | 167–206 | ~13 wine-analog drinks (Champagne, shrub, port, dessert-wine styles) |
| Ch.5 — Etcetera | 207–236 | Beer, milkshake, tea, horchata, and other non-cocktail formats |
| Index | 237+ | |

Each recipe is built from **sub-components** ("stocks," "syrups," "batches") that can be made ahead and refrigerated, mirroring how the restaurants mise-en-place a service. A finished cocktail is usually: `[stock/tea/juice extraction] → [batch/base — sweetened & acid-balanced] → [to portion/assemble & serve]`. Every backbar recipe yields ~1 qt (1 L); most cocktail batches yield ~1 qt and are portioned to order.

---

## 2. Technique & Philosophy Synthesis

The 16-page opening essay ("A Culinary Approach to Beverage Design") is the intellectual core of the book. Key ideas:

- **Alcohol removal breaks balance, not just flavor.** A daiquiri is rum+lime+sugar in perfect balance; removing rum leaves an acidic, cloying drink. Water fills the *volume* but none of the rum's *function* (flavor carrier, viscosity, burn, sweetness counterweight). The design task is replacing alcohol's *roles*, not its taste.
- **Design axes considered for every drink:** Occasion (time of day, food pairing, portion count), Flavor & Aroma (borrow from the culinary world, not just the bar canon — mole, salad, oatmeal, fried chicken), Visual Aesthetics (glassware sets expectations independent of contents), Seasoning & Balance (adjust acid/sugar/salt/bitterness separately, as in cooking — most recipes intentionally under-season the base and season to taste at the end), Texture (mouthfeel — grippy tannins vs. thin/cleansing), Form Factor (should a flavor be presented as a "cocktail," a "wine," or a "beer"?), Serving Size (assertive flavors get smaller pours), Temperature (chilling mutes flavor, so cocktail-format drinks are over-seasoned to compensate), Ice (flavored ice cubes/spheres as a second flavor-delivery vector, borrowed from The Aviary's cocktail book), Clarity (clear = crisp/cleansing; opaque = mouth-coating; milk-punch and consommé-raft clarification techniques are used throughout), Proof/"heat" (alcohol's burn is sometimes simulated with chili, ginger, etc., though the authors admit it's hard to fully replicate).
- **Structural techniques repeated across the book:** sous-vide (or DIY water-bath) infusions at 90°C/195°F for extracting spice/herb/fruit flavor without alcohol; milk-punch clarification (curdling warm milk with acid, straining through the curd bed); consommé/egg-white raft clarification (savory technique repurposed for a sparkling white-chocolate-guava drink); Pectinex/kieselsol/chitosan fining for crystal-clear juices; carbonation via Perlini shaker, home soda carbonators, or CO2/N2O cartridges (including a triple-carbonation "widget" simulation for a stout); flavored ice molds (spheres, cubes) as a secondary flavor-release mechanism.
- **Equipment called out as important:** digital scale (grams throughout — most recipes are weight-based, not volume), Vitamix-grade blender, electric juicer (centrifugal or masticating), Perlini carbonating shaker or a hand soda-carbonator, ice molds, Japanese jiggers.
- **Practical/safety notes:** fresh-ingredient NA drinks can spontaneously ferment (produce real alcohol) if left at room temp — refrigerate, use within ~a week, freeze for longer storage. Standard batch yield is ~1 qt/1 L ("enough to fill a large bottle").

---

## 3. Recipe Sample — Organized by Section

All recipes below are transcribed with exact quantities/ratios/method as printed.

### A. Basic Syrups (p. 22)
**Simple Syrup:** 100 g sugar + 100 g warm water, whisk to dissolve, refrigerate.
**Demerara Syrup:** 200 g demerara sugar + 100 g water, heat until dissolved, cool, refrigerate.

### B. Back Bar — "Non-Alcoholic Spirits" (yield ≈1 qt each)

**In the Style of GIN** (p. 28)
- Juniper tincture: 300 g vegetable glycerin, 150 g juniper berries, 150 g water — blend briefly, steep overnight refrigerated, strain.
- Botanical base: 750 g water, 17 g coriander seeds, 17 g lemon peel, 5 g fennel seeds, 4 g cinnamon sticks, 3 g fresh ginger, 2 g star anise, 1 g angelica root, 1 g green cardamom, 1 g kosher salt — sous-vide 90°C/1 hr or simmer 1 hr covered; strain, combine 250 g of this with all the juniper tincture.

**In the Style of AMERICAN WHISKEY** (p. 29): 60 g malted barley (oven-toasted 400°F/30 min), 1 banana (roasted whole, unpeeled, until skin blackens), 1500 g water, 75 g dried peaches, 75 g dried figs, 60 g popped popcorn, 45 g charred oak chips, 37 g fenugreek seeds, 34 g molasses, 30 g cinnamon sticks, 2 vanilla beans, 1.5 g salt — combine roasted components + remaining ingredients, sous-vide 90°C/1 hr or simmer 1 hr, strain.

**In the Style of TEQUILA** (p. 30): ½ pineapple (bark only, charred flesh reserved elsewhere), 1000 g water, 40 g apricot, 30 g agave syrup, 20 g roasted peanuts, 12 g black peppercorns, 2 g salt, 2 vanilla beans, 50 g parsley, 25 g ginger, 15 g mint, 9 g lime peel, 7 g jalapeño, 4 g bay leaves — simmer bark+core spices 1 hr, strain, add remaining fresh aromatics, steep 2 hrs room temp, strain again.

**ORANGE LIQUEUR** (inspired by Triple Sec/Cointreau, p. 31): 650 g whole oranges incl. peel (quartered), 550 g water, 450 g sugar, 2 cloves, 4 g cinnamon, 1 vanilla bean — sous-vide 90°C for 4–6 hrs until oranges are very soft, strain.

**In the Style of MEZCAL** (p. 32): charred pineapple bark, 1000 g water, apricot, agave syrup, 25 g liquid smoke, peanuts, coriander, black cardamom, black/white/Szechuan peppercorns, salt, 2 g lapsang souchong tea, parsley, ginger, jalapeño, bay leaves, lime peel — char pineapple bark on grill, simmer with spices 1 hr, strain, steep with fresh aromatics 2 hrs, strain again.

**In the Style of SPANISH RUM** (p. 33): caramelize 60 g sugar, add 3 vanilla beans, 25 g orange peel, 7 g cacao nibs, 3 g nutmeg, 2 g salt, deglaze with 1000 g water, steep 1 hr, strain. *Variant — Blackstrap Rum:* stir in 80 g molasses after cooking.

**ORANGE BITTERS** (inspired by Regans', p. 34): 500 g water, 50 g vegetable glycerin, 25 g gentian root, 20 g coriander, 20 g dried orange peel, 8 g star anise, 5 g cloves, 5 g green cardamom, 4 g cinchona bark, 1.5 g caraway — sous-vide/simmer 1 hr, strain.

**AROMATIC BITTERS** (inspired by Angostura, p. 35): burnt sugar (40 g) base + cloves, cinnamon, gentian root, star anise, dried orange peel, nutmeg, cardamom, ginger powder, allspice, black pepper, vanilla, sassafras/sarsaparilla, 1000 g water — toast spices in burnt sugar, deglaze, simmer 1 hr, strain.

**BITTER AMARO** (inspired by Cynar, p. 36): 1000 g water blended with 500 g whole artichokes, oxidized uncovered 2 hrs (turns black), strained; 700 g of this liquid added incrementally to caramelized 200 g sugar, plus 200 g Coca-Cola, 36 g basil, gentian root, cinchona bark, tarragon, angelica root — simmer 1 hr, strain.

**In the Style of JAMAICAN RUM** (p. 37): roasted bananas + charred pineapple bark, water, lime peel, ginger, toasted oak chips, black cardamom, allspice, cinnamon, salt — sous-vide/simmer 1 hr, strain.

**AMARO** (inspired by Averna, p. 38): caramelized sugar (300 g) + French chicory, cinnamon, coriander, angelica root, fennel seeds, gentian root, anise seeds, cloves, water, orange peel — simmer 1 hr, strain.

**In the Style of SWEET VERMOUTH** (p. 39): caramelized sugar + verjus rouge, vanilla, ginger, dried figs/cherries, orange/lemon peel, raisins, cinnamon, coriander, cardamom, gentian root, cloves, chamomile, cinchona bark, tarragon — simmer 1 hr, strain.

**HERBAL LIQUEUR** (inspired by Chartreuse, p. 40): honey, vegetable glycerin, poblano peppers, ginger, mastic, basil, parsley, galangal, coriander, star anise, fennel pollen, fennel seeds, tarragon, cardamom, thyme, orange/lemon peel, black peppercorns, cinnamon, fenugreek, mint, Szechuan/green peppercorns, cloves, saffron — sous-vide/simmer 1 hr, strain.

**In the Style of FERNET** (p. 41): burnt-sugar base + galangal, cane syrup, fresh & dried mint, aloe vera leaf powder, gentian root, cinchona bark, green cardamom, saffron, water — simmer 1 hr, strain.

**BITTER LIQUEUR** (inspired by Campari, p. 42): 700 g water, 400 g sugar, fresh grapefruit & orange (incl. peel), gentian root, cinnamon, pink peppercorns, star anise, angelica root, cloves, optional red food coloring — sous-vide/simmer 1 hr, strain.

### C. Classic Cocktails (non-alcoholic riffs, using Back Bar components)

**GRAPEFRUIT MIMOSA** (p. 48): 1 oz fresh grapefruit juice, chilled + 4 oz "Lychee Champagne" (p. 194). Build in flute.

**PALOMA** (p. 49): 3 oz NA tequila, 1 oz fresh grapefruit juice, ¾ oz simple syrup, ¾ oz fresh lime juice, grapefruit peel. Shake with ice, strain over ice, express peel.

**DAIQUIRI** (p. 50): 3 oz NA Spanish or Jamaican rum, ¾ oz fresh lime juice, ¾ oz simple syrup, grapefruit peel (expressed, discarded). Shake, strain up.

**JUNGLE BIRD** (p. 51): 2½ oz NA Spanish/Blackstrap rum, 1½ oz fresh pineapple juice, ¾ oz NA bitter liqueur, ½ oz simple syrup, ½ oz fresh lime juice, maraschino cherry + orange peel garnish. Shake, strain over ice.

**MARGARITA** (p. 52): 3 oz NA mezcal, 1 oz NA orange liqueur, ¾ oz fresh lime juice. Salt rim, shake, strain up. *Variant — With Hot Sauce Ice* (p. 53): freeze Fresno-chili juice + water + lime + sugar into ice cubes; serve margarita mix shaken/strained over the spicy ice.

**ABSINTHE / DEATH IN THE AFTERNOON** (p. 55–56): Absinthe syrup = 250 g vegetable glycerin, 24 drops sweet fennel oil, 13 drops lemon oil, 6 drops star anise oil, 1 drop green food coloring (blended). Classic drip: 10 g syrup + 80 g very cold water, added slowly (louches cloudy). *Death in the Afternoon:* ½ oz absinthe syrup + 6 oz "Lychee Champagne."

**FRENCH 75** (p. 57): 3 oz NA gin, ¾ oz fresh lemon juice, ¾ oz simple syrup, shaken and strained into a flute, topped with 3 oz Lychee Champagne, lemon peel garnish.

**WHISKEY SOUR** (p. 58): 3 oz NA American whiskey, ¾ oz fresh lemon juice, ¾ oz simple syrup, 1 egg white — dry shake, shake with ice, strain, express lemon peel.

**BLACK MANHATTAN** (p. 59): 2 oz NA American whiskey, 1 oz NA amaro, 2 dashes NA aromatic bitters. Stir with ice, strain, garnish 2 maraschino cherries.

**AMARO & COKE** (p. 60): 2 oz chilled Coca-Cola + 1 oz NA bitter amaro or fernet, built over ice.

**BIJOU** (p. 61): 1 oz NA gin, 1 oz NA herbal liqueur, 1 oz NA sweet vermouth. Stir, strain.

**NEGRONI** (p. 62): 1 oz NA gin, 1 oz NA sweet vermouth, 1 oz NA bitter liqueur, orange peel. Stir, strain, express peel.

### D. Modern Cocktails (original restaurant creations)

**CEREAL KILLER** (p. 68–69, Manhattan-inspired): Blackberry syrup (200 g blackberries + 150 g sugar + 100 g water, boiled, strained). Spiced barley stock (toasted malted barley + cinnamon + allspice + water, steeped 1 hr, strained). Batch: 475 g spiced barley stock, 110 g blackberry syrup, 70 g POM pomegranate juice, 70 g Seedlip Spice 94, 30 g carob syrup, 17 g demerara syrup, 10 g Fee Bros. Black Walnut Bitters, 1.5 g citric acid, 0.5 g salt. Serve: 4 oz over a large ice chunk.

**WHAT WOULD HONEYDEW** (p. 70–73): Clarified lime cucumber (English cucumber juice + limes + angelica root + Pectinex/kieselsol/chitosan, clarified over hours). Mint honeydew ice spheres (honeydew juice, glucose, verjus blanc, peppermint extract, frozen into ½" spheres). Batch: 335 g aloe vera juice, 205 g clarified lime cucumber, 135 g verjus blanc, 95 g sugar, 1.5 g smoked salt solution, 0.5 g orange flower water. Shaken briefly, double-strained over the mint-honeydew ice.

**SALAD** (p. 74–75): Marinated grape tomatoes (blanched/peeled, marinated in olive oil, sherry vinegar, garlic, black pepper). Walnut paprika syrup (toasted walnuts, sugar, water, smoked paprika). Batch: 400 g fresh green tomato juice, 150 g verjus blanc, 100 g walnut paprika syrup, 100 g water, 26 g green olive brine. Served 3 oz, garnished with a marinated tomato, basil leaf, sea salt flake.

**SHAKE YOUR TAMARIND** (p. 76–77, designed for a spice-forward tasting menu): Tamarind coriander stock (toasted coriander/cinnamon + tamarind + water). Batch: 315 g tamarind coriander stock, 150 g fresh pineapple juice, 120 g water, 115 g Seedlip Garden 108, 30 g sugar, 25 g Maguey Sweet Sap, 20 g kalamansi purée, 2 g citric acid, 1 g salt.

**SPARKLING PLUM SOUR** (p. 78–81): Fruitcake ice cubes (spiced dried-fruit reduction frozen into cubes). Falernum (toasted cinnamon/star anise/allspice/cloves + cashews + ginger + vanilla + lime zest, sugar syrup). Plum verjus (sous-vide plum + verjus blanc + sugar). Assembly: 1 oz plum verjus, ¾ oz falernum, ½ oz fresh lemon juice, 1 oz water, 2 dashes aromatic bitters — carbonated via Perlini/soda device, poured over fruitcake ice.

**KOREAN SPICED MARGARITA** (p. 82–83): Ginger syrup, gochujang syrup (mandarin purée, sugar, yuzu marmalade, gochujang, citric acid), toasted barley stock, chili-salt-sugar rim mix. Base batch: 400 g barley stock, 200 g gochujang syrup, 135 g Maguey Sweet Sap, 70 g demerara syrup, 60 g ginger syrup, 37 g rice wine vinegar, 3.5 g red yuzu kosho. Serve: 4 oz base + ½ oz lime juice, thrown with ice, poured into a chili-salt-rimmed glass over ice.

**JUNIPER RAISIN** (batch on p. 99, carbonated, gamey-food pairing): Raisins + juniper berries steeped twice in boiling water, then citric acid/sumac/salt/sugar added, carbonated; served 3 oz over ice.

**GOLDEN GLOW** (p. 100–101): Black pepper stock (toasted peppercorns steeped in water). Batch: 455 g coconut water, 125 g fresh yellow bell pepper juice, 65 g fresh yellow tomato juice, 65 g aloe vera juice, 60 g passionfruit purée, 35 g black pepper stock, 20 g sugar, 10 g red miso, citric + malic acid. Served 3 oz, illustrating how clarity/settling changes mouthfeel (bright fresh vs. clarified overnight).

**NEW MILLENNIUM** (p. 102–105, 20th-Century-cocktail riff): Lapsang souchong tea → smoke ice cubes (with citric/malic acid, red food coloring). Nectarine white-grape stock (sous-vide). Base: verjus blanc, sugar, rose hips, nectarine-grape stock, rose water. Assembly: 4 oz base, 1 oz lemon juice, ½ oz water, ¼ oz simple syrup, carbonated, served over 2 smoke-ice cubes.

**ROASTED SWEET POTATO** (p. 106–107, served warm): Pomegranate green tea, fig stock, roasted sweet potato purée (potato + sweetened condensed milk, blended). Batch: 360 g fig stock, 160 g roasted sweet potato purée, 120 g pomegranate green tea, 30 g dark brown sugar, 25 g pomegranate molasses, citric acid, salt — warmed and served 3 oz with expressed orange peel.

**SPARKLING WHITE CHOCOLATE & GUAVA CONSOMMÉ** (p. 108–109): A savory consommé-clarification technique (egg-white "raft") applied to a sweet liquid. White chocolate (96 g) melted into water/ice/vanilla/treated guava purée (guava purée + Pectinex, rested 4 hrs), combined with whisked egg whites (50 g), slowly simmered to form and clarify through the raft, strained through cheesecloth. Carbonated batch: 530 g consommé, 230 g water, 26 g sugar, lactic + citric acid. Poured 4 oz, forms a beer-like foam head.

**MUM'S THE WORD** (p. 110–112, watermelon/licorice/ginger/chrysanthemum): Chrysanthemum tea, star anise stock, fig stock, fresh ginger juice (starch settled out). Batch: 1 large thumb ginger (juiced), 571 g fresh watermelon juice, 54 g chrysanthemum tea, 51 g fig stock, 37 g star anise stock, 25 g sugar, lime zest, malic acid, salt — infused with lime zest 2 hrs, strained, served 3 oz.

**THAI FIGHTER** (p. 114–115): Thai stock (toasted sesame/coriander + lemongrass, ginger, kaffir lime leaves, Thai chili — steeped 12 min). Batch: 350 g Thai stock, 125 g fresh pineapple juice, 75 g fresh grapefruit juice, 65 g sugar, 50 g fresh lime juice. Shaken with ice, double-strained, garnished with a grapefruit twist.

**SMOKED STRAWBERRY OLD FASHIONED** (p. 116–117): Mint tea, lapsang souchong tea, strawberry syrup (juiced strawberries, optionally Pectinex-clarified overnight, + sugar). Batch: 480 g Seedlip Spice 94, 216 g strawberry syrup, 76 g lapsang souchong tea, 36 g mint tea, 32 g demerara syrup, 24 g Fee Bros. Black Walnut Bitters. Served 4 oz over a large ice chunk, expressed orange peel inserted into the glass.

### E. Wines Chapter (Champagne/shrub/port/dessert-wine analogs)

**GRAPEFRUIT JICAMA CHAMPAGNE** (p. 170–171, aka "Lychee Champagne" used throughout the Classic Cocktails chapter): White grape juice infused with rose hips, basil, grapefruit peel (steeped stages), combined with fresh grapefruit juice, fresh jicama juice (for tannic "grip"), verjus blanc, citric acid — carbonated. Served 3 oz in a tall flute. Jicama starch simulates a Champagne's structured mouthfeel.

**TRUFFLED CHERRY** (p. 172–173, red-wine style): Truffle oil stock (7 g black truffle oil infused into 200 g water via sous-vide, oil later drained off, water retains aroma). Batch: 215 g water, 215 g tart cherry juice, 185 g truffle oil stock, 80 g Maguey Sweet Sap, 55 g bergamot purée, 25 g sugar, salt. Served warmed to room temp, 3 oz.

**GRAPEFRUIT TURMERIC SHRUB** (p. 174–175): Grapefruit vinegar (sugar-cane vinegar infused with grapefruit, turmeric, tarragon, dried hibiscus, sous-vide 70°C/4 hrs). Batch: 610 g water, 76 g grapefruit vinegar, 49.5 g sugar, 10.5 g vegetable glycerin, citric acid, salt. Served 3 oz — designed as an acidic white-wine analog to pair with food.

**BANANA CHAI** (p. 176–179, milk-punch clarified "white wine"): Banana chai tea, roasted bananas, cocoa powder, lactic acid — blended into a smoothie-like base, then clarified by curdling with warmed milk (milk-punch technique), rested 1–2 days for full clarity, sweetened with sugar/salt. Served 3 oz, resembles a crisp white wine once clarified.

**CARDAMOM PORT** (p. 180): visual/style note — a rich, viscous port analog (photographed being poured into a decanter-style glass).

**SMOKED ICE WINE TEA / 3-SPICE VINEGAR / SPICED BLUEBERRY SHRUB** (p. 181–189): 3-spice vinegar (star anise, cinnamon, apple cider vinegar, black vinegar, dried pear, Szechuan peppercorns, sous-vide 70°C/4 hrs). Shrub batch: 570 g water, 90 g 3-spice vinegar, 45 g smoked ice-wine tea, 30 g blueberry juice, 25 g sugar, 20 g vegetable glycerin, malic acid. Served 3 oz.

**ROOM FOR DESSERT?** (p. 182–183, coffee/orange/chocolate digestif): Coffee chicory stock (coffee beans, chicory, licorice root extract, bitter orange peel, steeped). White chocolate syrup (melted white chocolate + sugar, fat-separated over ice, strained). Batch: 225 g water, 225 g coffee chicory stock, 150 g blood orange purée, 150 g white chocolate syrup, citric + ascorbic acid. Served 2 oz — a Sauternes-style sipper meant for dessert/cheese course.

**ROSE CHAMPAGNE** (p. 184–186): dried rose petals infused into a stock to introduce both color and a dry, "sandpapery" tannic mouthfeel; recipe is tunable — more rose stock/less sugar = drier/more tannic (three versions photographed at different intensities).

### F. Etcetera Chapter (non-cocktail formats)

**UMAMI BOMB** (p. 205, savory, pairs with mushroom dishes): Black garlic purée (12 g black garlic blended with 300 g water, strained). Batch: 225 g water, 160 g Concord grape juice, 150 g verjus rouge, 100 g black garlic purée, 45 g cane syrup, 20 g fresh beet juice, 14 g low-sodium tamari, salt. Served warmed to room temp, 3 oz.

**BREAKFAST STOUT** (p. 208–209): Steel-cut oats, cinnamon, chocolate malt, black barley toasted and simmered with water, raisins, cacao nibs, hop pellets — strained, then lactose (70 g, for creamy mouthfeel), powdered dark malt extract, dark brown sugar added. Triple-carbonated: 2× N2O charges (for a Guinness-widget-style silky texture) + 1× CO2. Served 5 oz.

**OLIVE OIL** (p. 210–211, pairs with cacio e pepe): Herb stock (lemongrass, tarragon, mint, meyer lemon peel, thyme, vegetable glycerin, sous-vide 75°C/30 min). Stabilizer powder (gum arabic + xanthan gum). Batch: 522 g herb stock, 150 g verjus blanc, 44 g sugar, salt, 1 g stabilizer powder, 22 g extra virgin olive oil — oil emulsified in by blending at low speed while drizzling in. Served 3 oz; note that the emulsion will separate over time and should be re-shaken/blended.

**CHICHA MORADA** (p. 212–213, Peruvian purple-corn drink): Toasted coconut flakes. Base batch: 600 g water, 200 g purple corn, 200 g fresh pineapple (skin on), 95 g fresh lime, 30 g cinnamon sticks, 25 g toasted coconut, 2 g cloves — simmered/steeped, strained. Cocktail: 1½ oz chicha morada base, ¾ oz fresh pineapple juice, ¾ oz fresh lime juice, ½ oz simple syrup, 1 egg white, 1 dash aromatic bitters — dry shaken, shaken with ice, double-strained, garnished with 5 drops of bitters.

**TZATZIKI** (p. 218–219): Mint tea, cucumber mint stock (blended English cucumber + water, clarified overnight, citric acid), cardamom lime stock, cardamom yogurt (nonfat Greek yogurt + honey + sugar + lactic acid + salt), cucumber ribbons. Assembly: cucumber ribbon lining the glass, 3 oz cucumber mint stock + 2 oz cardamom yogurt over ice, mint sprig garnish, stirred with the straw.

**$5 SHAKE** (p. 214–217, Pulp-Fiction-inspired milkshake): Tonka saffron ice cream base (classic egg-yolk custard: 3 egg yolks, 75 g sugar, salt, 5 g tonka beans, 300 g heavy cream, 100 g buttermilk, 1 g saffron — cooked sous-bain-marie style to 155°F/70°C, strained, chilled). Sassafras milk (root-beer-flavor infusion: milk + sassafras/sarsaparilla root, simmered, sweetened). Whipped cream (cream + powdered sugar + salt, whipped or N2O-charged). Assembly: 450 g ice cream base + 100 g sassafras milk, frozen via liquid nitrogen / ice cream maker / manual freezer-scrape method into a thick shake, topped with whipped cream, grated tonka bean, and a Luxardo cherry.

**HONG KONG MILK TEA** (p. 220–221): Chicory tea + rose tea blend (Rose Noir + English Breakfast tea + rose hips) + evaporated milk + sugar. Batch: 470 g rose tea blend, 155 g evaporated milk, 85 g chicory tea, 55 g sugar. Served 3 oz.

**LEMON HORCHATA** (p. 222–223): Lemon oleo saccharum (3 lemons peeled, peels muddled with 100 g sugar, rested 4+ hrs, then dissolved with hot water). Lemon pepper syrup (oleo saccharum + Marconda almonds, white peppercorns, blade mace, blended and strained) — built toward a Spanish-tiger-nut-style horchata base, served over ice.

---

## 4. Notable Cross-Cutting Techniques Worth Highlighting

1. **The "Back Bar" concept as reusable infrastructure** — the 14 non-alcoholic spirit analogs (Gin, American Whiskey, Tequila, Orange Liqueur, Mezcal, Spanish/Blackstrap/Jamaican Rum, Orange Bitters, Aromatic Bitters, Bitter Amaro, Amaro, Sweet Vermouth, Herbal Liqueur, Fernet, Bitter Liqueur) are designed to be interchangeable building blocks, reused across dozens of downstream cocktails — the same design pattern as a real bar's back bar.
2. **Milk-punch clarification** (Banana Chai) and **egg-white raft/consommé clarification** (Sparkling White Chocolate & Guava) are savory/classical techniques deliberately repurposed for sweet, "wine-like" clarity.
3. **Flavored ice as a second act** — fruitcake ice, smoke ice, mint-honeydew ice spheres, hot-sauce ice cubes — are used throughout to let a drink evolve in the glass, replicating a technique from The Aviary's alcoholic cocktail program.
4. **Weight-based (gram) measurement** dominates component recipes; only the final "to assemble and serve" step uses oz/jiggers, since that's the format home bartenders expect.
5. **Carbonation is treated as optional but preferred** — nearly every fizzy drink gives three alternative methods (Perlini shaker / home carbonation device / no equipment, just shake-and-serve flat) so recipes remain accessible without specialty gear.

# Zero Proof Cocktails — 90 Non-Alcoholic Recipes for Mindful Drinking

**Autora:** Elva Ramirez
**Fotografia:** Robert Bredvad · Food styling: Mallory Lance · Prop styling: Maeve Sheridan
**Editora:** Houghton Mifflin Harcourt, Boston/New York, 2021
**ISBN:** 978-0-358-21191-4 (hardback) / 978-0-358-21346-8 (ebook)
**Design do livro:** Claudia Wu · Edição de receitas: Susan Choung · Teste de receitas: Abeo Miller (no bar do Katana Kitten, NY)

Arquivo fonte: `C:\Claude\vvf\LIVROS\Zero proof cocktails  90 non-alcoholic recipes for mindful drinking (Ramirez, Elva, author) (z-library.sk, 1lib.sk, z-lib.sk).pdf`

---

## 1. Visão estrutural

O livro tem duas metades desiguais: um ensaio histórico-cultural longo (~40 páginas) e um receituário de 90 drinques organizados por **perfil de sabor**, não por técnica nem por base alcoólica-substituta.

### Sumário

| **The Zero-Proof Guide to At-Home Drinks** (Tips on Technique and Products; Reducing Waste and Using Leftover Ingredients; No-Proof Bartender's Tool Kit) | 14–17 |
| **A Brief History of Temperance** — The Colonial Era to the Late Nineteenth Century | 20 |
| — Late 1800s to Prohibition | 27 |
| **The Mocktail Era** | 33 |
